Main Tab

 Click the options below for more Information

 At Parts and Materials View

 At Patterns and Parts View

    Return

The Return option returns you to the main S2M CENTER screen.
Keystroke

   Parts

This is the default opening view for the S2M CENTER.

You will notice in the figure above that the "Finished Left End" has a Red icon next to it on the Left. Any parts in the list denoted with a red icon is a part that will not be optimized. In this case the part is wider than the 48" wide
Material that is specified. An icon that displays just a Router icon, such as the "Nailer" part, indicates that this Part is not tagged to Optimize. An icon that displays both the brown sheet and the yellow router, such as the "Finished Right
End" part, indicates that this Part is tagged for both Optimize and CNC. See Right Click options later in this topic for more information.
To find the exact meaning of a particular Part icon simply hover over it to receive a description.
By default all items that can be sent to the Optimizer and/or your CNC machine will be sent to them. However, if you just wanted to send certain items to the Optimizer and/or CNC machine you can select them individually. To select
a part simply click the icon next to the part. To select more than one simply hold the Ctrl key down while selecting. You can also select a range of parts by selecting the first part in the list you would like to send and then hold the Shift
key down and select the last part. This will select all parts in between the first and last parts selected.
You can also change the Material of any part in the list. Simply click on the Material Name and a drop down will appear and allow you to change the current Part to any other Material available in the current PNC file.
Any item in the list can be modified. You can change the Quantity of parts, the parts size, you can even add an Overage using the Over column.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 2 of 365

If you have created a Machine Set you will notice the second column in this view is the Sel column. This column indicates which Machine set the Part will be outputted to. This is a quick method to compare. In the figure below I have
three possible Machine Sets, Manual (which is simply Manual output), Saw Set, and Router Set. The column, in order, will show either a | or a .
|.. indicates the Part is going to be output with Manual but not Saw Set or Router Set.
|.| indicates the Part is going to be output with both Manual and the Saw Set.

                     The red arrow in the view above is there to let you know you can add a new Part to this Part list.

      Selection Copy

       There are a couple of important features to note when working in List Views throughout S2M CENTER.
       When in a List View is possible to copy text from one cell into multiple cells by grabbing the origin cells copy handle and dragging it up or down into other cells. See the example illustrated below.
       Here I have several Adjustable Shelves where I would like their Descriptions to all be Adj Shelf. Normally I would need to type the Description for each or use normal Windows Copy and Paste functionality.

       Using the Selection Copy functionality I can hover near the bottom right corner of the origin field and will get the copy handle as shown below.

       Then simply drag to the last cell I want to copy to as shown below.

       Then simply let go of the click and you will be prompted to accept the changes.

       Click Yes and you will see the change.

                     Cells which are automatically populated or that must be unique will not allow this functionality.

      Cell Hover Hints

       When hovering on a cell which includes a button for selection you will see an outline of the button. Clicking on that outline will activate the button and clicking outside that outline will activate the field. Notice below I am
       hovering over the first Material field in the illustration below.

      Right Click Options

       Right clicking on a Part in the Part list brings up the following menu:

       There are six possible commands available in this Right Click menu:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                    Page 3 of 365
                                                                                                                                                9/25/2026
          Copy - Creates a Copy of the selected Part.
          Edit
          Delete - Clicking the Delete command will delete the selected part from the Parts List.
          OK to Optimize - This option allows you to determine if the selected Part is OK to go to the Optimizer/Nester.

                 If you do not see this option check to make sure Show Parts Not Set for Optimization or Output is enabled in Preferences.
          OK to Output CNC - This option allows you to determine if the selected Part is OK to go to your Secondary Machine.

                 If you do not see this option check to make sure Show Parts Not Set for Optimization or Output is enabled in Preferences.
          Save to Library - Clicking the Save to Library command will save the selected part to the Part Library for use in future Jobs.

      Return

 The Return option will prompt to Save your Part and return you to the main Edit View.

        Clipboard

 The Clipboard option has three tools:

                 Cut

         The Cut option allows you to "Cut" selected objects to the Windows clipboard for pasting.

                  Copy

         The Copy option will allow you to "Copy" selected objects to the clipboard for pasting.

                  Paste

         The Paste allows you to "Paste" an object stored on the Windows Clipboard onto your Part.

 See the Zoom Group topic for more information.

There are two options in the Edit Shape Layer selector:
       Shape - The default Layer and the one all Shapes must be drawn on.
       Construction - This Layer allows you to draw Construction/Reference Lines to assist in creating your shape.
                     Depending on which Layer is active some options in the Modify section of the Ribbonbar may or may not be available.

      Select

This option will change the cursor to an arrowhead and allow selection of lines, arcs, etc. After selecting the item, you will be able to change properties by selecting the desired property off the list in the sidebar and selecting the item to
apply it to. Lines can be moved, resized, etc.

        Select All

This option will Select All objects in the Shape Editor.

        Measure

This option allows you to Measure Objects.

       Delete

This option will allow you to Delete the selected CAD Object(s).

         Transform

                     The Transform tools will only be available (not greyed out) when selecting objects which are not Constrained.

              Flip

       This option will Flip the selected object. First select all of the CAD Objects you would like to Flip using the CAD Selection Options.

       Offset

This option allows you to Offset a copy of the selected Object a specific Dimension. Simply click the Offset option in the Ribbonbar then select (Left Click) the CAD Object you would like to Offset.
You can enter the Offset Dimension in the sidebar...

...or drag your cursor the amount (distance) you would like to Offset.
In the following example, the chamfer line has been offset by ¾" and is being offset again be 2 ½" as noted in the Offset field on the left. The dashed line parallel to the chamfer is where the offset will land when the left mouse button
is clicked.

      Crop

There are 2 main Crop options in the sidebar once the Crop Tool is selected:

Crop Type - You have three Crop Types available; Rectangle, Circle, and Polygon that determine the shape of the area being cropped.

Crop Area - The Inside option will get rid of what is Outside the Boundary and keep what is Inside. The Outside option specifies that you want to keep what is outside the Crop Boundary and get rid of the objects inside the
Boundary.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                      9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                            Page 11 of 365

      The Inside option can be a bit aggressive as it will remove all Objects in the current view if it is not inside the boundary. To keep this option from being so aggressive you can limit the Objects that are affected by a
Crop by selecting the Objects first.

          Remember you can always click Undo option in the Quick Access Toolbar or use the  +  Undo shortcut to Undo your changes.

          Remember you can select multiple Objects by holding the                         key on the keyboard down as you click the Objects.

Bend

This tool allows you to select a Line and turn it into an Arc by dragging it to Bend it.

Line

This tool allows the creation of Lines to define the shape of your Part. After selecting and placing a line, the line properties window will display in the sidebar. Clicking on the items listed in this window will allow modification of that
item.

           Chained Line

This tool allows the continuous creation of connected Lines.

        Line Box

This tool allows the creation of a Rectangle.

       Arcs

                      Arc by Radius

       This option allows definition of a point where a radius begins, the radius, and the degrees of radius. The first mouse click marks the center of the radius, pull the radius that you want and click again.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                               9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                    Page 12 of 365

         A circle will appear with a radius line within it. Move the cursor and the circle opening becomes an arc as you do so.

When you reach the correct position, a third click will set the arc at the selected point.

              3 Points Arc

This option lets you place two points to define the opening of the arc, and the third point pulls the arc increasing the radius. A 3 Points Arc is very handy when you have three known points to Snap to.
In the example below I want to create the Arc shown in red.

To begin I select the Arcs|3 Points Arc option from the Ribbonbar then Left Click the End Point (With Snap To Points on) of the first line.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                       9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 13 of 365

       Next I Left Click the Mid Point of the second line.

       And the last and third point I Left Click the End point of the third line.

       And now I have the Arc.

This option will check your Assembly or Part Shape to make sure it is valid. Objects that are valid will be Green and objects which are not will be Red. Usually a Shape is judged as invalid if it is not a complete enclosed shape. See
illustrations below for examples.

Edgeband

The Edgeband Editor allows you to assign Edgebanding to your Parts. You must have Edgeband Materials and have setup Edgeband Indicators in the Material Catalog in order to fully utilize this facility.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                     9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                            Page 16 of 365

The Edge Banding option allows you define which edge of a Part gets edge banded with which edgeband indicator. Once the Edge Band option is active you will be placed in Edge Band mode with the following 4 options in the
ribbonbar.

  Select - When entering Edgeband mode this tool is selected by default. Simply click on the edge you would like to apply the Edgeband to and then select its type in the sidebar.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                               9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                    Page 17 of 365

          You can use Windows  + Click to select multiple edges.

          Select All - To quickly change all Edges to a specific Edgeband select this option then select their type in the sidebar.

          Remove Banding - Will remove Edgebanding from the selected edge(s).

          Set Banding - Will expose the Edgeband type selector in the sidebar without having to select an edge first.

It is important to note that the default values in the sidebar options are NOT representative of the current Edge Banding on the selected Part. To use this function simply select which Edgebanding type you would like.
Once the Banding options are chosen simply select any of the Part Edges you would like these Edgeband properties assigned to. In the image below the Front Edge of the End has been selected.

Note that the front edge is now red which indicates an Edgeband has been applied. Once you are through defining the Edges simply click the Return  option to save the new Edge Properties.

         Line Bore

 When selecting the Line Bore option you will left click to establish the start point of your row of Line Bores then drag and left click to establish the end point.
         When selecting a Line Bore operation there are thirteen options in the sidebar Properties area:

       Pos X - Establishes the X Position of the start point for the Line Bore.                                                                                       9/25/2026
       Pos Y - Establishes the Y Position of the start point for the Line Bore.
       Pos Z - Establishes the Z Position for the Line Bore.
       End X - Establishes the X Position of the end point for the Line Bore.
       End Y - Establishes the Y Position of the end point for the Line Bore.
       Length - Establishes the Length of the Line Bore.
       Diameter - Establishes the Line Bore Diameter.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                            Page 19 of 365
                                                                                                                                                         9/25/2026
       Depth - Establishes the Line Bore Depth.
       Angle - Establishes the Line Bore angle. Rotation occurs around the start point.
       Spacing - Establishes the Line Bore spacing.
       Repeat - Establishes the number of Line Bores.
       Tool - Allows you to assign a specific tool to the Line Bore.
       Description - The Description of this Line Bore.

       Hole

 When selecting the Hole option you will left click to establish the center-point of the Hole.
         When selecting a Hole operation there are seven options in the sidebar Properties area:

       Pos X - Establishes the X Position of the start point for the Hole.
       Pos Y - Establishes the Y Position of the start point for the Hole.
       Pos Z - Establishes the Z Position for the Hole.
       Diameter - Establishes the Hole Diameter.
       Depth - Establishes the Hole Depth.
       Tool - Allows you to assign a specific tool to the Hole.
       Description - The Description of this Hole.

      Circle

This option allows you to create Circles. Click in the place where you want the center of the circle and pull the mouse to one side or the other and a Circle will develop. The area in the sidebar allows modifying the radius by
numerically entering a value.

     Tool - Allows you to assign a specific tool to the Circle.

         Rectangle

This tool provides for creating a Rectangle (or square) by clicking the point where it is to start and clicking on the point where it is to finish. It can thereafter be adjusted by using the Select tool. The area in the sidebar is similar to the
one for the Line tool.

     Angle - Establishes the Rectangle angle. Rotation occurs around the start point.
     Type - There are two choices for Type:

            Outline - Will cut an Outline of the Rectangle.
            Pocket - Will Pocket out the Rectangle.
     Cut - There are three options for Cut:
            Centerline - Cuts along the Centerline of the Rectangle.
            Inside - Cuts along the Inside of the Rectangle.
            Outside - Cuts along the Inside of the Rectangle.
     Tool - Allows you to assign a specific tool to the Rectangle.
     Description - The Description of this Rectangle.

Edit Shape Selection Options

 There are several methods to select Edit Shape objects as well as several functions you can perform on Edit Shape Objects once they are selected.

 Left Click Select

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                         Page 26 of 365

Simply Left Click on a single CAD Object to select it.

Left Click Select +

To select multiple CAD Objects simply hold the          key while Left Clicking.

Left clicking +           on a single CAD entity and selecting a CAD node for it will also put that item into Scale/Stretch/Move mode just like using the multi-select options.

Window Selection

You can also Left Click the screen and drag a Window to enclose all of the CAD Objects you want to Select.

Selecting Single Objects

Left Clicking +           on a single entity and selecting a node for it will also put that item into Scale/Stretch/Move mode just like using the multi-select options. See the Selecting a Group of Objects section below for

more information on Scale/Stretch/Move.

Selecting a Line

There are six options when selecting a Line.
     Start X - Allows you to set the Starting X position of the selected CAD Object.
     Start Y - Allows you to set the Starting Y position of the selected CAD Object.
     End X - Allows you to set the Ending X position of the selected CAD Object.
     End Y - Allows you to set the Ending Y position of the selected CAD Object.
     Length - Allows you to set the Length of the selected CAD Object.
     Angle - Allows you to set the Angle of the selected CAD Object.

Selecting an Arc (Arc by Radius)

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                            9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                             Page 27 of 365
                                                                                          9/25/2026
 There are five options when selecting an Arc (Arc by Radius).
       Center X - Sets the Center X position of the selected CAD Object.
       Center Y - Sets the Center Y position of the selected CAD Object.
       Begin Angle - Allows you to set the Begin Angle for the selected CAD Object.
       End Angle - Allows you to set the End Angle for the selected CAD Object.
       Radius - Allows you to set the Radius of the selected CAD Object.

 Selecting an Arc (3 Point Arc)

 There are eleven options when selecting an Arc (3 Point Arc).
       Start X - Previously covered
       Start Y - Previously covered
       End X - Previously covered
       End Y - Previously covered
       Arc Point X - Allows you set the Arc Point X position for the selected Arc.
       Arc Point Y - Allows you set the Arc Point Y position for the selected Arc.
       Center X - Previously covered
       Center Y - Previously covered

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm

       Begin Angle - Previously covered
       End Angle - Previously covered
       Radius - Previously covered

 Selecting a Group of Objects

There are eight options when selecting a Group of CAD Objects:
     Left - Sets the Left position of the CAD Object.
     Right - Sets the Right position of the selected CAD Object.
     Top - Sets the Top position of the selected CAD Object.
     Bottom - Sets the Top position of the selected CAD Object.
     Center X - Previously covered
     Center Y - Previously covered
     Width - Sets the Width of the selected CAD Object.
     Height - Sets the Height of the selected CAD Object.

There are several options when selecting CAD Objects you can initiate by simply clicking points on screen around the selected CAD Object(s).

Scale

Once a Group of CAD Objects is selected simply left click one of the corner points to Scale the selected Objects. You either eye-ball the Scale or you can use the Tab key to change the values in the sidebar for the selected objects.

Stretch

Once a Group of CAD Objects is selected simply left click one of the mid-points to Stretch the selected Objects. You can either eye-ball the Stretch or you can use the Tab key to change the values in the sidebar for the selected objects.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                        Page 29 of 365

              You can also Stretch to a Snap point. For example you can pick a single line and grab and end-point and stretch/extend that line to any other Snap point.

Move

Once a Group of CAD Objects is selected simply left click any point within the object to use that as the Move point, this will allow you to Move an Object or Group of Objects from one known (snap point) position to another (snap
point position). You can also Move the selected Group of Objects by using the Tab key to change values in the sidebar.

              Since Scale and Stretch are also available by clicking the corners of selected CAD make sure the proper cursor prompt is used.
                  Scale/Stretch
                     Move from End Point

              When using Copy and Paste on CAD Objects, upon Pasting the Objects, they remain selected to allow you to use the three (Scale, Stretch and/or Move) options described above.

Object Snapping

The S2M CENTER provides a special kind of cursor snap in addition to the typical grid snap. This allows the cursor to automatically snap to different hot-points on or adjacent to another object; for example, end-points, mid-points,
center-points, and intersections. These locations are commonly referenced when creating or editing geometry. For most functions, the cursor will also snap to the origin of the object.

       For example, you may want to create a line that is connected to the end of an arc. Start creating the line by locating the endpoint of the arc. When located, a round hot-point marker appears on the arc endpoint.

         Click the hot-point marker to start the line there. The start of the line snaps precisely to the end of the arc, connecting these objects end-to-end.  9/25/2026
 You can choose what types of positions and conditions will automatically be located by enabling or disabling options in Preferences | Tools.

 General Snapping Shortcut Keys

 Ignores snap to Grid and Points, and leaves the Smart-Lines angle snap on.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                 Page 30 of 365

Activates the "full grid snap" mode, forcing snap to the Grid over a position just aligned with a Point.

Completely ignores snap, including Smart-Lines.

Dynamic Snapping

The S2M CENTER also has the ability to Snap Dynamically (also known as Assumptive Snapping). When you are in line with a Snap Point on another Object within the Drawing space the S2M CENTER will project that position to
your location. When this projection along with the associated hot-point marker is displayed you can be assured that the Object will be placed in-line with that Objects Snap Point.

       For Example you would like to draw two line Parallel to each other. After drawing the first line simply move your cursor in-line with the first lines Start Point and you will see a Snap Line projection assuring you they are in-line.

 Hot-Point Markers                                                                                                                                                       9/25/2026

 When the cursor moves over a valid snap location, the located position displays with a hot-point marker and a special cursor appears to identify the type of location.
               The system will not display a hot-point marker for a particular location type if it is disabled in Preferences | Tools.

 The list below identifies these special cursors:
         Cursor is snapping to one of the extremities (End Point) of an object.
         Cursor is snapping to the middle position (Mid Point) of an object.
         Cursor is snapping to the center of an arc or circle.
         Cursor is snapping to the intersection of two objects.
         Cursor has one coordinate aligned with any of the snap points.
         Editing CAD line is extending along the original angle.
         Editing CAD line is snapping to one of the smart line angles.

   Materials

This changes the View to a Material List. You can modify the Material, Sheet Size, Cost, and Quantity of materials in this list.

             The red arrow in the view above is there to let you know you can add a new Material from the S2M Material Catalog, or a copy of a Material within the current Job with an alternate size, to this Material list.

Right Click Options

Right clicking on a Material in the Material list brings up the following menu:

There are 4 commands available in this Right Click menu:

1.              Delete - Clicking the Delete command will delete the selected material from the Materials List.

2.              Remove Tool Override - If you have set an alternate Tool as the Outline Tool, via Material Properties below, for this Material there will be a small green tool on the Materials icon. This lets you know that a Tool

    override is present on the Material. Clicking this option will remove that override allowing the S2M CENTER to select the proper Outline Tool based on your Tool Catalog settings.

3.              Remove Override - If you have modified the Optimization/Nesting Properties for a specific Material either from this menu or from the Patterns Area sidebar there will be a check mark on the Materials icon. This

    lets you know that an override is present on the Material. Clicking this command will remove that override allowing the Material to be Optimized/Nested utilizing the Machines default properties.

4.              Properties - Clicking the Properties command will bring up the optimization/nesting properties for the selected material. This works like an override. The Properties you change here will be for the selected Material

    only. This will allow you to re-optimize with different results and not adversely affect the other materials for this PNC file.

Machine Set

Machine Sets allow you to group together Machines you have created in the Machine Catalog. Machine Sets are specifically designed for an "automated" factory where operations are being performed in line on multiple machines and
operations are being consumed until no operations remain. Machine Sets are also a very flexible solution even if you are not running a fully automated factory allowing you to set machine sets to focus certain Materials, Part Types,
Operations, etc... onto multiple machines using the Filtering capability. You can also use Machine Sets to send Programs to different Machines and based on workload decide which machine to work them through (see Example 3
below).

               Example 1

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 43 of 365

               Let's say you have a Saw, D&D, and a P2P. The P2P has 5mm Horizontal Bores and the D&D has 8mm Horizontal Bores.
               A Part with only 8mm Horizontal Bores will never get to the P2P because the D&D will consume them first.
               A Part with both 5mm and 8mm Horizontal bores will get a program for the D&D which consumes the 8mm Horizontal bores and then get a program for the P2P which consumes the 5mm Horizontal bores.
               Example 2
               If you had three machines where machine 1 has 3mm HBores, machine 2 has 5mm HBores, and machine 3 has 8mm HBores.
               A Part with 3mm, 5mm, and 8mm HBores would have programs generated for all 3 Machines.
               Example 3
               Let's say you have a Saw and 2 Point-to-Point Machines and you want to spread production load across the 2 Point-to-Point machines and let the shop make the decision for which Point-to-Point to run the programs on.
               Simply add your 2 Point-to-Point Machines to the Machine Set with your Saw and enable the "Operations Pass Through" option for the first of the two Point-to-Point Machines and programs will be created for the job for
               both Point-to-Point Machines.
Having each Machine within a Machine Set uniquely Tagged is extremely important especially to get good workflow reporting. The workflow string is the series of machines the part needs to move through. The workflow string is
available in xLabel and for Reports in the PSNC-xx.mdb. It combines the machine tags of each machine in the workflow process some examples are SAW-DND-P2P, SAW-(PP1/PP2)-(DD1/DD2). Machines in () means the part can
be milled from any machine in the brackets. If a part normally can go to a (group) but not all the operations can be milled at each machine in the group then the machines that can't do all the operations will be eliminated. If the only
way to get all the operations milled in a (group) is to mill some operations at one machine and other operations at another then the machines will no longer be grouped and only the 1st instance of the operation will be milled.
See the Workflow topic for more information.
When selecting a Machine Set you have the following options on the Machine tab:

       Tag - This is a short name for the currently selected Machine Set. This option helps a machine operator identify which Barcode on a Label goes with which Machine (Setup). For example the following three examples
       demonstrate how it is used (displayed). The tags are highlighted in yellow.
       Label for one Secondary Machine

       Same Barcode Sent to Two Different Machines (Machine Set)

       Different Barcode to Two Different Machines (Machine Set)

       Available - Determines whether the Machine Set is available for Output.
       Machines In Set - This area is where you assign Machines to your Machine Set. A Machine set can only have one Primary Machine but any number of the other Machine Types. Once you have a Primary Machine included the --
       BAND PARTS-- option is added to the list and can be used to establish when in the Machine Set process edgebanding is applied to your Parts. This lets the Machine Set know which Machines need to be compensated for
       Banding and which do not. Those Machines afer the --BAND PARTS-- sequence need to be machined with Edgeband included.
       Available Machines - These are all the Available Machines in your Machine Catalog. Simply highlight the Machine you want to add to the Machine Set and click the left arrow button to add it.
       Operations Pass Through - The default action of a Machine Set is to consume operations and once consumed not be passed to the next Machine. With this option enabled the Operations will pass through to all the Machines
       within the Machine Set. This is a good solution if you want to output programs to multiple Machines and then allow the shop to choose which machine they want to run the programs on.
       Filters - Any Filters you have saved can be applied to a Machine Set in the Filters area. Simply select your saved Filter from the selector (highlighted yellow below) then click the Add button to make it part of the Machine Set.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 44 of 365

Workflow

Objectives

   1. Allow for different work flow scenarios where operations may be milled on multiple machines.
   2. Report work flow for a particular part.
   3. Disable warnings of an operation could not be performed on a part if it can be picked up by another machine down the chain.
   4. Provide a fail-safe program for damaged parts.

Manual (non-Machine Set) Workflow

In a Manual (non-Machine Set) Workflow scenario, operations start at the Primary Machine (Saw/Nest). If an operation can be performed on the machine then it is marked as being complete and is not available on the next
machine. This works great with the following machine setup.

The following shows how the operations would be divided for this part with the nest creating the outline and vertical operations. The D&D taking the boring on the cardinal edges and the P2P getting whatever is left over.

Work Flow Option #1a

More than one P2P machine available: In this scenario, after the part is cut at the Saw or Nest the part could be brought to either P2P for finishing. All operations that were still present after the Saw/Nest would be performed on
either machine:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                            Page 45 of 365
                                                                                                                         9/25/2026
               If the operations are completely consumed prior to the next machine the work flow would be complete

 Work Flow Option #1b

 This is a similar scenario except that the D&D machine is used prior to the P2P

               In this scenario if there are no D&D operations, that machine is skipped.

 Work Flow Option #1c

 Another variation of option #1 with multiple D&D machines, you could use either one

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 46 of 365

Work Flow Option #1d

Final variation of option #1: Multiple D&D and P2Ps available. You can decide where to mill the part based on current machine availability.

Work Flow Option 2

This scenario account for having a line of D&D machines, each doing specific tasks. Unlike the previous scenario where the P2P operations went to each machine, in this case the D&D operations would be consumed by a specific
machine and not repeated on another.

Work Flow Option 3

In this scenario maybe you never want to mill any bore holes on the Nest machine. You want to do all boring on the secondary. However, all other operations are to be performed by the primary machine.
When the S2M Center encounters a situation like this where operations have been designated on a machine that is further down the work flow chain, we don't want any error/warning messages about the operations not being performed
on the Nest machine.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                              Page 47 of 365

User Interface

The Machine Set interface will show the order in which the Machines would be processed. Each Machine would decide if the operations are consumed by that Machine or pass-through to the next Machine. These two icons is used to
give you some feedback as to whether the operations being consumed or pass-through:

The icon on the left indicates a pass-through. Any operation performed on this Machine will still be available on the next Machine.
The icon on the right indicates a stop. Any operation performed on this Machine will consume the operation and it will not be available for any other Machine is the set.
Here are some sample setups for scenarios listed above:

All Stops: Each machine consumes any operation it performs

Since we want the operations to be valid for either P2P, we allow the operations to pass-through P2P-1, however, we consume them on P2P -2 so they don't get passed down to the D&D.

This is close to the traditional output except it can go to either P2P machine; therefore we allow all operations to pass-through P2P-1.
              If we had set the D&D as a pass-through, the horizontal boring would have been passed to the P2Ps.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                 9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 48 of 365

By setting D&D 1 as a pass-through, you can do the horizontal boring at either D&D 1 or 2. Note that D2D 2 is set to stop, otherwise the horizontal boring would have been passed along to the P2P machine.

This is setup so that either D&D can be used, and either P2P can be used on what is left over. Note that we put a stop on the last of each machine type.

Since each D&D does specific operations, we don't want any pass-through to occur on the D&Ds, each has been set to a stop. However, whatever operations are left over can go to either P2P so we set the P2P-1 as a pass-through so
any leftover operations from D&D would be available for P2P-2 as well.

Even though we intend to have the boring output on the P2P we DO NOT want to set the Nest as a pass-through, this would cause all the other operations (route, dado, and outline) to pass-through to the P2P as well. So this would be a
typical "no pass-through" setup with the boring holes assigned to the P2P machine by assigning them to a ToolID that was only available on the P2P.

Special Situations

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 49 of 365

In a modification to the above scenario, we have the boring assigned to a ToolID that is available on each P2P machine. We have set both P2P1 and P2P2 as pass-through so all operations are available on any of the P2P machines.

In this scenario we have setup some special large boring tools on P2P 1. We want those operations done with those boring bits and we don't want them passed along to P2P2 & P2P3 so we set a stop after P2P1.

The Safety Net

This scenario has a normal workflow, but it has added a safety net by 1st outputting to a "Full Outline" P2P machine that is set to pass-through. Since it is a pass-through all operations will go onto the nest as normal, but if the part is
damaged for any reason, a new part can be cut to an approximate size and using the barcode for the 1st P2P machine you can completely Re-Mill the damaged part.

Pitfall - Not all paths produce the same results

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                            Page 50 of 365

The S2M Center can anticipate scenarios where due to the machine limitations an operation can only be performed on one of the two P2P machines. In this case you really wouldn't have the option to take the part to either P2P
machine.

It is suggested that at any shared set of machines, if ALL the machines cannot perform the same amount of operations then you eliminate that path. Using the following arrangement as an example:

If D&D 1 could perform more operations that D&D 2 then we would only output to D&D 1 for this part. But you could still use either P2P.
If P2P 2 performs more operations that P2P 1 then P2P 1 is not acceptable output either. The only work-flow for this part is Saw/NestàD&D 1à P2P 2

Program Flow Diagram

 +Label - Label Group

 See Sheet Router

 +Label - Image Group

 See Sheet Router

 Optimization - Complexity Group

       Optimization Quality - Controls the Quality of the Optimization of your Parts.
             Fastest - This setting offers the fastest Nesting at the computer, sacrificing Yield.
             Fast - This setting offers a slightly slower speed at the computer improving yield slightly.
             Good - This setting offers a balance between speed at the computer and Yield.
             Better - This offers slower speed at the computer with increased improvement to Yield.
             Best - This offers the slowest speed at the computer with the best Yield results sacrificing speed at the computer.

       Booking Priority - Sliding to the left favors Yield (less books) while sliding to the rigfht favors Labor (more books).
                       Make certain to set up Booking for your Saw in the Machine Catalog Setup Tab.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                      Page 60 of 365

 Max Phases (Turns) - Sets the maximum number of Phases your Saw can handle.

                Most older saws have a maximum of 3 phases. Make sure to check with your Saw Manufacturer to find out how many Phases (Turns) your Saw will support.

 Eliminate Z-Cuts - Allows you to eliminate Z-Cuts. A Z-Cut is the "Third Phase" cut on any given strip. A "Z-Cut" and "Third Phase Cut" are the same as long as they are cut from the 1st strip set in a pattern. However, we
  allow the strip series to change directions a maximum of 6 times, the Z-Cut could occur on the 3rd, 4th, 5th,6th, 7th, or 8th phase. Allowing Z-Cuts can cause a dramatic improvement in Yield but at the cost of time spent on the
  Saw. This can cause the operator to make multiple cuts to bring a part to its finished size.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                         9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 61 of 365

                Eliminating Z-Cuts will affect the type of patterns that are generated but it will not reduce the phase count. Make certain to set up the Phase Count for your Saw in the Machine Catalog Setup Tab.

 Eliminate T-Cuts - Allows you to eliminate T-Cuts. T-Cuts are cuts made to break a sheet apart prior to Ripping and/or Cross-Cutting. Allowing T-Cuts can cause an improvement in Yield but like Z-Cuts can also cost time
  spent on the Saw.

 Part Size Tolerance - Depending on possible unit conversion variances during transfer to the S2M Center from your design/engineering product you may have parts you consider the same size but may have a slight variance in
  size. This allows you to set a Tolerance for which the S2M Center will consider the Parts the same size.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                     Page 62 of 365

Parameters Group

     Min pos of crosscut fence - Allows you to establish the Minimum Position of the Crosscut Fence with the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     Height of blade over mat - Allows you to establish the Height of the Saw Blade over the Material on the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     Max pos of rip fence - Allows you to establish the Maximum Position of the Rip Fence with the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     Max l/w ratio for X-cut - Allows you to establish the Maximum Length/Width Ratio for cross cuts on the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw. Set this to match the setting on your

       Saw.

            Excerpt explaining the Ratio value from Altendorf:
               If the ratio length / width of the part exceeds a value of 4 or 5, its width has to be cut at the rip fence, otherwise the crosscut fence could give you no exact 90 degree angle.
               Examples:
                    · The material is sized 700mm x 500mm, the workpiece has to be 660mm x 480mm:
                                           Both cuts are performed at the crosscut fence.
                    · The material is sized 1200mm x 200mm, the workpiece has to be 1150mm x 190mm:
                                           The length cut of 1150mm is done with the crosscut fence, but the width of 190mm is cut at the rip fence, because the 200mm are too narrow to give an exact 90 degree position for the
                                             cut. Therefore the postprocessor parameter "ratio length/width workpiece".
                                           Some identical parts have to be cut out of the rest of a stripe: For instance 7 identical parts have to be cut. For 6 parts the rip fence gives the measure, the 7th part is turned around and
                                             cut at the crosscut fence. An exception might be that the 7th workpiece is smaller than 146mm or the length/width ratio forces the part to the rip fence.

     · Favor rip fence for rips - Allows you to establish that you favor the Rip Fence for Rip cuts with the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     · Favor rip fence for X-cut - Allows you to establish that you favor the Rip Fence for Cross Cuts with the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     · Favor rip fence for last - Allows you to establish that you favor the Rip Fence for your Last Cut with the Altendorf Elmo or Martin T74 Saw Link. Set this to match the setting on your Saw.
     · Allow Negative Waste - This option will appear when a Casadei CN04, CPOUT, Gabbiani Prima, Homag Espana CH03, Macmazza SEZ2000, Macmazza Tork MP70, SCMI Sigma 65, SCMI x915, SCMI z32, or SCMI z45 NC

       Links are selected. Negative Waste can be created when Grain Matching.

            Each cut in the figure below is considered a "Gap Cut"; a gap cut is a cut required to maintain the gap between 2 parts. This machine's kerf is set to 1/4". Therefore the cut instruction for the 3/4" gap cut would be a 1/4"
               waste piece. When part 1A is cut it leaves a 1/2" gap before 2A. To remove the 1/2" the machine must cut a 1/4" piece of waste (1/4" waste + the 1/4" kerf = the 1/2" gap).

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 65 of 365

 In the figure below the 1/2" and 3/8" gap cuts are considered Negative Waste. Negative waste is output red because it requires special saw instructions. Using the logic described in Gap Cuts, the 1/2" gap requires a 0" cut
    and the 3/8" gap requires a 1/8" cut. Since the typical saw controller cannot accept either a 0 or negative cut these have to be handled with special cutting instructions. Since only some saws can process negative waste this
    feature is turned off by default. For saws that cannot process these instructions the negative waste gap cuts are reduced to equal the saw kerf. This would affect the 1/2" and 3/8" gaps above, but not the 3/4" gap.

The following Selco XML parameters can be populated with information from the Parts table, Materials Table and the PartRunData table. Information about each of these can be found in the psnc-ac-cv-cw-nc & +Label
Databases topic.
To define these parameters values, note the table you want the information from and then note the name of the Field name that you wish to use. To add the information to Draw_1 use the following syntax {Table Name.Field Name}.
Example Image name would be written as {PartRunData.ImageName}
Any field with a value of 0 will output a blank.

Intelli-CAM - Tool Movement

     Compensation Type - (Compensation refers to an adjustment in the path of the tool to compensate for a change in tool size due to tool wear. This is referred to as Tool Comp. or Tool Compensation). See the document on
       Output Path for more information.
               Tool Center No G41/G42 - Tool Comp is accommodated strictly though the tool path. It is offset from the actual cut path and doesn't look to a register in the machine's control to adjust the path. The outline path of the
               part is larger than the part's geometry by the tool radius. This path is to be followed by the center of the tool.

 Tool Center G41/G42 - The outline path is identical to Tool Center no G41/G42. The difference is it triggers to look up tool compensation values on the machine's control. Tool comp is important when you start
    sharpening tools. When you sharpen a tool it gets smaller. Tool comp allows you to inform the control that the bit has changed size and to compensate for the change. For example: If you use a ½" router bit (.25 radius) and
    as a result of the bit being sharpened it has a new size (.23 radius). You need to account for this tool size change somewhere.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                 Page 86 of 365

           With Centerline - Tool Center no G41/G42 you would go back to the Tool Catalog in the S2M Center and enter the new size of the tool (.46 diameter) and re-output the program. Otherwise your part would cut out
              larger than it should be.

           With Centerline - Tool Center G41/G42 you can enter a value of .02 at the machine's control (.25 - .23 = .02) and the machine's control will offset the tool path by .02 and correctly cut out the part.

           It boils down to user preference whether the sharpened tool size is accounted for in the S2M Center or at the machine's control although you have advantages at the machine since if you need to swap bits you can
              compensate for different sizes on the fly rather than having to re-output from the S2M Center.

 Tool Radius - This path follows the outline of the part exactly. Thus if the router were to follow the path centerline, the part would be too small. This path must be compensated for at the machine's control. If you are using
    a 1/2 bit, the machine's control will need an offset of .25 (radius). If you sharpen the bit you would need to change the comp value at the machine's control to (.23). In either case the machine's control will offset the path of
    the tool by the tool radius resulting in the part being milled to the correct size.

       Force Centerline on Open Interior Paths - Forces open Route paths inside the Part to centerline.  9/25/2026

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                        Page 87 of 365

 Nesting - Outline Group

       Outline Start Point - This option allows you to specify the Outline start point type.

  There are three options here:

       Center - The standard S2M start point along the mid point of the edge of the Part.

       Perimeter - An innovative S2M Center start point methodology. The concept is cutting the parts from the outside of the sheet first moving towards the center with the point of origin always being towards the center of the
          sheet. Using this pattern each part is attached to a very large section of the sheet when its outline is cut so that its holding power is not just from the size of the part but the size of all other uncut parts. We have found this to
          be a excellent method with superior results for minimizing part movement with smaller Parts in most cases not requiring any special small Part Handling (Onion Skins, etc...). There are several other options which are
          available below when this Outline Start Point type is selected.

       Corner - An alternative start point method for use on Machines where witness marks on along the start/end points are a problem. This method leads in and out at the corners of the part rather than the mid point of the edge.

 Outline Sort Type - This option allows you to control the general order which Parts are machined in the Nest.

         There are three options here:                                                                                            9/25/2026

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                        Page 98 of 365

       Size - Sorts by size smallest to largest. This is a good strategy to avoid Part movement making sure the smaller Parts are cut first while the maximum vacuum is available.
       Position - Follows a logical sequence around the sheet.
       S Pattern - Follows an S Pattern on the sheet.

 Decelerate At End Of Outline - If you want the tool to decelerate at the end of the outline you can set the value (measurement) here for when the deceleration should begin. This can minimize Part shifting on the final release of
  the Part.

 Percent of Feed Rate - The percentage of the current feed rate you would like to use on the decelerate above.

 Large Part Area - Once defined, any large parts are removed from this routine, they are cut last since they are large enough to have sufficient hold down in themselves. This will increase the hold down of all other parts in the
  process.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                           9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 99 of 365

 Auto-Skin Center Parts - With this feature on, any small part or very small part will automatically get a return onion skin if it is placed in the center of the sheet. Since the center of the board is cut last these parts have a greater
  possibility for movement. With this feature on you can have Ramp-In/Out set for all parts types and only those parts susceptible to movement will receive a return onion skin, optimizing both part hold down and overall cycle
  time.

 Link Part Outlines - This option allows you to turn on Part Bridging in your Nests. Bridging is when all Parts within the Nest are cut with a single Lead-In and Lead-Out for the Sheet. This option can improve Sheet cycle time
  by reducing the number of times the Tool Lead In/Out on each individual Part.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026

Nesting - 6th Face Group

     Allow 6th Face Output - When using this option you will be able to actually machine your Secondary (Flip Side/Face 6) operations on the reverse side of the Sheet prior to machining your Operations and Cutting out your Parts
       on the Primary Face. This option will use your outline tool to clean up one long edge and one short edge of your sheet (by one half of your Trim Cut) to give you a clean edge for alignment to 0/0 after flipping the Sheet to finish
       your nest.

                       When Nest Programs are output they will have one of three suffixes helping the CNC Operator identify 6th Face Output.  9/25/2026
                     N = Nested sheet with no corresponding 6th face program.
                     A = The back-side (6th face) program to run before Z program.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 105 of 365

              Z = Main program with nest operations and part outlines; just like N.

 Rotate 6th Face Trim - Depending on which corner of your machine your 0/0 Reference Pins reside you may need to rotate the trim cuts that are run along one long and one short edge. This options allows you to control where
  these cuts are performed.

Nesting - Common Line Group

     Common Line Cutting - This option will turn Common Line Cutting on or off. Common Line Cutting output tightly packs the parts in a nest so that the outline path can cut two parts with a single pass. Although this type of
       output has a better yield on material and allows for a longer tool life it has its downsides. A toolpath only has one clean edge. Based on the tools rotation and the material climb vs. conventional cut the S2M Center instructs the
       tool to cut around the parts in the optimal direction. With common line cutting there is no way to do this. With a common cut one part is going to get a clean edge and the other will experience some blowout. You should be
       working with sharp tools to minimize the blowout. In addition common line cutting uses a centerline path with no compensation. It is very important to have the correct tool size in the Tool Catalog to ensure the parts are the
       correct size. For this reason it is recommended that common line cutting only be used for non-essential parts and less than premium Materials. Because of this the common line settings are both per machine and per material
       based.

 Common Line Maximum Retrace - This value will affect the final tool path for the outlines. If you specify a retrace value that allows the outline path to move alongside a path that has already been cut resulting in fewer rapid
  moves. You should base this preference on how fast you machine transitions from rapid to machine moves.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 106 of 365

 Common Line Retrace by Small Parts - If you are allowing retracing you may want to ensure that retracing does not occur near small parts since they may have shifted after being cut. The S2M Center automatically avoids
  retracing near very small parts. This setting will ensure that retracing also avoids the small parts. Setting this value true will result in more rapid moves so it should be set to false if you are not having problems with your small
  parts shifting.

       Offcuts.

Small Parts - Tabs & Skin Group

     Tabs - Tabs can be employed to help hold a "Small" or "Very Small" Part. They are areas where the router cuts at less than full depth for a short distance during the lead in thus leaving a "bridge". The lead out of the router
       removes this "Tab" over the area. During the time the router is cutting the Part free, the Tab provides the security that can help keep the Part from shifting.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                              9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                            Page 114 of 365

 Onion Skin Thickness - Allows you to set the Onion Skin Thickness when using the Onion Skin Lead Type.
 First Pass Offset - If you are using an Onion Skin Pass this option allows you to have the first pass cut the Part oversized (rough cut) by the specified amount.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                               9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                     Page 115 of 365

Small Parts - Lead Types

     Lead Types - The manner in which the Tool enters and exits the operation. There are five Lead Types:
                  1. None - A simple tool plunge where the bit plunges straight into the Material (least favored). Cutter Compensation will not be applied.

          2. Sloped - The most common Lead Type. This lead allows for less strain on the bit and provides cleaner machining of the Material. Sloped is also a required Lead on most Machines if Cutter Compensation (G41/G42)
             is being utilized. The Cutter Compensation is actually applied by the Machine during the Lead In.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                        9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 116 of 365

          3. Tab - Tabs can be employed to help hold a "Small" or "Very Small" Part. They are areas where the router cuts at less than full depth for a short distance thus leaving a "bridge". A second pass of the router removes
             this "Tab" over the area. During the time the router is cutting the Part free, the Tab provides the security that can help keep the Part from shifting.

          4. Onion Skin - Typically used for small parts, the path defining the part shape is cut to a depth slightly less than the material thickness. After the first pass, there remains a thin "Onion Skin" of material that anchors
             the part in place. The router then repeats the path, this time removing the onionskin, and freeing the part.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                            Page 117 of 365

          5. Return Onion Skin - Similar to Onion Skins, except ALL Return Onion Skin parts receive the first pass prior to any Return Onion Skin Part receives the second (or "liberating") pass.

 Small Parts - Nest Boundaries Group                                                                                                                                                                9/25/2026

       Boundary Shape Small Parts - There are several options here:

Specialized Drill and Dowel

 This Machine Type is specifically for Drill and Dowel Machines. Following are the Setup Tab options for Specialized Drill and Dowel:

 Setup Tab - Specialized Drill & Dowel

 The fields defined below will only be available if your Drill & Dowel supports the feature the field defines.

 Depth Match Group

       Face Up - This setting will force the program to be written either face up or down regardless of if the part is face up/down on the nest or saw.

              You can override the up/down flag on an individual part with the DNDFLIP parameter.
Face Up with Label-IT
Below is sample output for 2 different edge machines. Each machine has a different Face Up setting.
The arrows will indicate how the part should be fed into the machine and since this part was on the saw Face Down that the Face Up program(s) require the part to be flipped.
When two programs share the same name but different Face Up/Down requirements then multiple arrows will be shown to accommodate. The arrows will coincide with the machine tags field.
Note that when this part is BU (back up) it has a Left and Bottom program. When it is flipped Face Up, it has a Right and Bottom program. The L,R,B,T programs are always relative to the primary edge of the part and if
the parts is Face Up/Down.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                    9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 126 of 365

In the past the Label was always rotated so that the Arrow pointed to the primary edge of the part. However there was a disconnect with this rotation and how the part appears on the Label in Label-IT. In Label-IT the part
is always rotated 90 degrees since parts are typically longer then they are wide to maximize the part image.

Now the label is always rotated so that it points to a part length.

In the image depicted below we have the same part with 4 different primary edges. The primary edge corresponds with the part number. In the past the 2nd parts label would point to the right and the 4th parts label would
point left. In the new scheme the part with the primary edge of 2 or 4 have the same rotation as a part with 1 or 3 respectively.

Note that the position of the label has not changed, only the rotation.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 127 of 365

Silent Mirrored Programs

If a part is shaped there may be difficulty lining up the part on the fence of the machine on its shaped side. We now output a silent mirrored program for all shaped parts. The mirrored program can be run on the opposite
fence if needed.

Note that the mirrored program will still have the same Face Up/Down orientation as the primary program. In essence only the X-Position is mirrored (not the Z).

These additional programs will only occur for shaped parts.

The Barcodes on the Label Image will be for the primary edge program. The operator will need to be trained/informed that there is an additional program if necessary.

The edge program has a new naming convention:

       Previously

       R010011L (Run Number R01) (PartID 0011) (Edge L = Left)

       New

       R01P00BL (Run Number R01) (Primary or Mirrored P/M) (PartID in base 36 00B) (Edge L = Left)

       R01M00BL (Run Number R01) (Primary or Mirrored P/M) (PartID in base 36 00B) (Edge L = Left)

       With Base 36, the three 3 characters reserved for PartID can now store 46,655 unique IDs from 000 to ZZZ

       If the part is shaped the program will output a R01M* program in addition to the R01P*

       All D & D output except for the Weeke BEK that describes the entire part now feature the mirrored programs.

       EXAMPLE OUTPUT

       R78P00DL:
               Edge Width
               4.50000
               Number Of Holes
               2
               Hole 1
               0.75000 1 0 1
               Hole 2

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                Page 128 of 365

                         1.25000 1 0 1

                 R78M00DL:
                         Edge Width
                         4.50000
                         Number Of Holes
                         2
                         Hole 1
                         3.75000 1 0 1
                         Hole 2
                         3.25000 1 0 1

Match Depth Explanation

       In the grid below I have defined 4 different Match Depths (Triggers) for each Operation Type. The dimension used here are a bit extreme. You would probably define them with about .001" difference when you actually
       utilize it.

Drill Match Depth  0.125

Dowel Match Depth  0.25

KD Match Depth     0.375

Glue Match Depth   0.5

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                   9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                            Page 130 of 365

                In this situation if I passed a Horizontal Bore Operation through the S2M Center that was 1/8" Deep the Machine would Drill Only.
                If I passed a ¼" Deep Operation it would Drill and Insert a Dowel. This would be used for inserting glue-less dowels.
                If I passed a 3/8" Deep Operation it would Drill and Insert a KD Fitting (If your Machine supports it, otherwise this field will not be available.)
                If I passed a ½" Deep Operation it would Drill, Inject Glue, and Insert a Dowel.
       Glue Duration (milliseconds) - Allows you to specify the Glue Duration on your Drill and Dowel Machine in milliseconds.

Koch Maxi 5

 This is a Chop Saw Machine Link.
 Output for Koch/Paul Maxi 5 Premium Control.
 Only selected parts made of board stock are output.

 Structure of Cutting List To Be Imported into MAXI 5 Premium Control

 The file to be imported must start with the order details comprising 9 lines, each with a maximum of 100 characters. This line will start with #; and end with ;
 Then follow the individual lists.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                      Page 139 of 365
                                                                                                                                     9/25/2026
 Each list may contain a 'project' line with a maximum of 100 characters. The project name will start with #; and end with ;
 Each list may contain max. 400 sizes.
 Data have to be in ASCII code, i.e. ASCII characters, hex 20 to hex 7E. Not allowed are: $ , @ ,\ .
 Decimals to be separated by a point, not by a comma, each line to end with <CRLF> (Carriage return / line feed).
 File name: nnnnnnnnnnnn.stk . Any name with the ending stk.
 Line structure:
 P xx; Active; Control; Length; Number of Pieces; Sorting Station; Priority; Printing Code; <CRLF>
 Thereby:
 P xx: Cutting list number 1 .. 60
 Active: If the lengths in this line are to be used, set this value to 1, otherwise to 0
 Control: For special applications, always set to 1
 Length: in millimeters with one decimal place, e.g. 1000.2 mm, min. 20.0 mm, max. 7000.0 mm
 Number of pcs. 1 to 5 000000
 Sorting station: 1 to 48
 Priority: 1 to 300 in %, standard value is 100%
 Printing code: If not used, then send " ;" (2 blanks and semicolon).
 Printing code may comprise max. 30 ASCII characters (no semicolon, no @ within the character string)
 Example: Test cutting list_220304.stk
 #; 'Order' line1;
 #; Client: Muller Timber;
 #; Species: Oak;
 #; Timber section 33*45;
 #; Completion. Xx.yy.zzzz;
 #; STATISTICS: 3345;
 #;
 #; Further information;
 #; Line 9;
 P1; #; 'Project' text cutting list 1, max. 100 characters;
 P1; 1; 1; 567.3; 125; 2; 100; 70.47.45 42 front jamb;
 P1; 1; 1; 825.6; 222; 3; 95; 10.39.45 651 rear jamb ;
 P1; 1; 1; 925.6; 322; 4; 105; 71.43.45 600 upright ;
 P2; #;'Project' text 2;
 P2; 1; 1; 456.7; 222; 4; 100; TTTT ;

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                    Page 140 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                                                   9/25/2026
P2; 1; 1; 625.6; 347; 3; 95; rail Q2 ;
P3; #; Additional components Q3;
P3; 1; 1; 555.7; 225; 2; 90; GHZF;
P3; 1; 1; 721.6; 1347; 9; 100; batten 567.34 ;
...
...
P60;

Omga T 421/521

This is a Chop Saw Machine Link.
Only selected parts made of board stock are output.

File Protocol T 421/521 OPT

The Structure must be the following
     `S' ; = start transmission
           1. `P' ; = cycle number (max 3 characters)
           2. `:' ; = (optional line) id , cycle alphanumeric name (max 8 characters)
           3. `X' ; = (optional line) n° box ( max 3 characters)
           4. `Y' ; = (if first grade, optional line; if omitted, grade =1) grade values = only 1 or 2
           5. `Q'...'T' ; = length (max 3 numbers+ 3 after the decimal sign), number of repetitions (Limit of 500 Records per File)
     `E' ; = end transmission

The cycles sent as per above will be written in the programs memory with the same rules as function FUN1; therefore no cycle number grater than the next cycle available will be accepted.
By transmitting cycle number already present in the controller, the cycle on the controller will be overwritten.
IMPORTANT:
Because of programming restrictions it is necessary to:

   1. close each line with a semi-colon';'
   2. start the line with the character identifying the data that will follow (see above)
   3. use only UPPER-CASE characters for the letters preceding the data on each line
Example of ASCII file with unit of measure Inches (controller memory empty) :

S         ;start transmission

P1        ;cycle #1

:first    ;id = `first'

X1        ;box # =1

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                      Page 141 of 365

Y1           ;grade 1
Q25.125 T5   ;piece length =25 1/8" , Number of pieces (repetitions) 5

P2           ;cycle #2
Q12.500 T8   ;piece length =12 1/2" , Number of pieces (repetitions) 8

P2           ;cycle #2
Y2           ;grade 2
Q10.750 T10  ;piece length =10 3/4" , Number of pieces (repetitions) 10

P3           ;cycle #3
Q30.750 T12  ;piece length =30 3/4" , Number of pieces (repetitions) 12
Q15.500 T5   ;piece length =15 1/2" , Number of pieces (repetitions) 5

E            ;end transmission

Note 1: new cycles begin each time the Material or Width changes

Note 2: in order to make the data sequence more understandable, it is allowed to:

    insert blank lines in between the data lines

    insert spaces (only after the numbers and not the TAB character)

    insert comments on each line after the semi-colon ';' character

Note 3: the unit of measures will be the one selected on the controller with the `UN_MIS' parameter

Note 4: by not transmitting the optional lines those field will be set to ZERO (i.e. ID, width, box, grade).

Pistorius BF-A

This is a Chop Saw Machine Link.

Output for Pistorius BF-A CNC Sawing and Notching (Haunching) Machine. Only parts made of board stock are output. Output can include machining mortise and tenons for stiles and rails when operations are linked to parts using a
specific tool (see the eSupport Tech Packages area for examples of using this NcLink with Cabinet Vision Solid).

Valid feed rates for the tool are 1 - 5 in/sec.

Output File Specifications

The are 3 sections in the file, Cabinets, Parts and Materials:

[Cabinets]
The Cabinets section is not used.
[Parts]

The Parts section has the following structure:
PartID - Unique identifier for the part (Data type: long integer)
CabinetID - Not used. Must be 0.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                         9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 142 of 365

PartName - Name of part (not the part type) (Data type: string 50 characters max)
MaterialID - Unique identifier from [Materials] section that defines the parts material (Data type: long integer)
PartTypeID - Identifies the part type (Data type: long integer, 1-Right style, 2-Bottom rail, 3-Left style, 4-Top rail, 5-Mid style, 6-Mid rail)
ParentID - Not used. Must be 0.
ChildID - Not used. Must be 0.
Side1Data - Notch locations (Data type: string 255 characters max, see notes below)
Side2Data - Notch locations (Data type: string 255 characters max, see notes below)
PartNumber - Not used. Must be 0.
Left - Not used. Must be 0.
Right - Set to length of part not including extra length required due to bead (Data type: string 16 characters max)
Bottom - Set to length of part not including extra length required due to bead (Data type: string 16 characters max)
Top - Always set to zero for your import file
Notes on format of Side1Data and Side2Data:
Each notch on the side has the following format
";MaterialID:NotchStart,NotchEnd;"
Each entry is surrounded by a semicolon. If no notching is required there will just be a ; in the field.
The MaterialID comes from Materials section with width of the material matching the width of the notch.

[Materials]

The Materials section has the following structure:
MaterialID - Unique identifier for the material (Data type: long integer)
MaterialName - Name of material (Data type: string 20 characters max)
NumberOfBeads - 0, 1, or 2 (Data type: integer)
Width - Overall width of material including bead dimension (Data type: string 10 characters max)
Bead - Size of bead (Data type: single)
CutSpeed - Speed material will be cut. (Data type: string 10 characters max,1-5in/sec 3 seems to work with everything)

This is a Chop Saw Machine Link.
Only parts made of board stock are output. Output can include scribe marks for positioning mid stiles and rails.

Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                      Page 143 of 365

Razor Gauge File Format

The following is the field information for the Razor Gauge Cutoff Saw.

Material Name
Material ID
Part Quantity
Part Name
Part Width
Part Length
Part Thickness
Room Name
Room ID
Cabinet Name
Cabinet ID
Scribe Marks

The scribe marks are identified in a string formatted using the pipe symbol "|" as the delimiter:

A part that needs scribe marks at 10, 12, 12.5, 18, and 19.75 would be output as:

`'10 | 2| .5 | 5.5 | 1.75''

A part that had no scribe mark would be " "

Currently the output is restricted to imperial

The data in the file is already sorted by:

   1. Material
   2. Part Width (descending)
   3. Part Length (descending)

Materials that are not board stock are not filtered out.

This is a Chop Saw Machine Link.

This link will create the .asc files that are used by the Salvador SuperCut Series of Chop Saws. There are a number of parameters that are used to generate the output for this link and details on what each parameter means is below.

JN                                Adds the job name to the file that is created for this output
LAB                               This is a label that will be applied to all parts, this can contain variable text (i.e. {PartName}). The full variable list will be provided at the end of this text file.
