PM                                Printing Mode (0 = None)
SP                                Speed (0 - 100%)
TCL                               Trim Cut Length (mm - in)
ECL                               End Cut (mm - in)
PRI                               Priority (0 = Length, 1 = Fixed, 3 = Value)
FO                                Fast Optimization
DMC                               Delete cut between two pieces if their sum is less longer than this value (mm - in)
SGT                               Shattering - If the waste is more than (mm)
SLT                               Shattering - and less than (mm)
SHA                               Shattering - cut it in pieces of (mm)
WO                                Cutting Waste Optimization
DQ2                               Disable quality from 2nd
WGT                               Waste Selection - If waste is more than (mm - in)
WEJ                               Waste Selection - eject it on ejector n

TpaCAD

This is a Drill and Dowel Machine Link.
This output will create program files for Drill and Dowel Machines using TpaCAD. The output supports Horizontal Drill / Glue / Dowel and Vertical Boring. Assign tools to this machine and to the boring operations to create output.
Additional instructions such as dowel, glue, macro name, programmable depth, and tool number can be set on tools assigned to this machine in tool catalog. One program will be created per edge.
If you need to use the pocket screw attachment for this link, create a new horizontal tool, then assign it to the machine using this link, then set the Macro property to pk.tcn (this is case insenstivite). When that is done, the neccessary
changes to the output will be made when the pocket screw attachment is needed.

Tool Properties

PSHOWTOOLPROP_ToolNumber

Link Parameters

     · Add Glue - (_hmGlue) [Bool/Tool]: Add glue when using this tool
     · Add Dowel - (_hmDowel) [Bool/Tool]: Add dowel when using this tool
     · Macro - (_hmMacro) [String/Tool]: The macro name to use for Glue/Dowel operation: forainsert.tmcr
     · Base zone # on Part Size - (_hmZoneOnPrtSize) [Bool/Machine]: Option to uset he smaller inside zones if the part will fit
     · Dimension 'A' - (_hmMaxSizeForZone) [Measurement/Machine]: Maximum size for the small zone
     · Programmable Depth - (_hmProgDepth) [Bool/Tool]: When true, this will output operation depth for this tool, when false, depth will equal 0

Specifications for Accu-Systems MTH variable

The MTH machine manufactures face frame and door components. These components are relational in their intersections, and units. This relational information is usually not provided in the standard CSV exports provided by design
software. This document is designed to outline the data, and format that is needed from the design software in order to process the data for proper machining on the MTH machine.

       A normal record from a CSV file would appear as follows:

Description,Width,Thickness,Length,Qunatity,Job,Material,Assembly,Comment1,2,3
Stile,2.5,0.75,29.5,4,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0,Profile1

The record provides items such as width, length, and other component specific data used for cutting and labeling. What is missing is any data related to an intersecting part like you would find in a door, or a face frame. Also profiling
data for the sides of each part is required to detail the component. For design software to coordinate this data, additional information is needed. A new variable that breaks down all relating intersecting parts, and profiling can be added
to provide this data. It requires several things:

   1. Unique Unit names for assemblies per job
   2. Unique part names per unit
   3. Center point intersection
   4. Side distinct referencing
   5. Profile name for Side 0
   6. Profile name for Side 1
   7. Profile name for End 0
   8. Profile name for End 1

An example of this would be:

Stile-1,2.5,0.75,29.5,4,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,(1~2~Rail-1|1~28.375~Rail-2),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1

Stile-2,2.5,0.75,29.5,4, JOB,Red Oak, Unit1,1,0, Profile15,(0~2~Rail-1|0~28.375~Rail-2),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1

Rail-1,4,0.75,52.875,2, JOB,Red Oak, Unit1,1,1, Profile1,,ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1

Rail-2,2.5,0.75,52.875,2, JOB,Red Oak, Unit1,1,1, Profile1,,ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1

In this example, each part within the unit is defined as Stile-1 or Stile-2 etc. within Unit1. The last field (0~2~Rail-1|0~28.375~Rail-2) references:

   1. The side of the part where the intersection occurs
           a. 0 references the left side of a vertical part
           b. 0 references the top of a horizontal part
           c. 1 references the right side of a vertical part
           d. 1 references the bottom side of a horizontal part

   2. The center point of the intersection
   3. The part name of the intersecting part
   4. "|" separates the intersections within the field

In this way complex face frames can be manufactured quickly and easily. The following section demonstrates in detail.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 153 of 365

With this example there are two stiles, rails, mid-rails, and one mullion. The naming nomenclature to make the two parts unique is unimportant as long as the names are different. In this example they will be specified with a "-1" or "-
2".

       The left stile would be detailed in the CSV as follows:
Stile-1,2.5,0.75,29.5,1,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,
(1~1.25~Rail-1|1~28.25~Rail-2|1~13~MidRail-1|1~18~MidRail-2),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1
Since all of the intersections of Stile-1 are on the right side, all references are "1". The rails (Rail-1-Rail-2) in this instance are 2.5 inches wide causing the intersection to be centered at 1.25 inches and 28.25 respectively.

       The right stile would be detailed in the CSV as follows:
Stile-2,2.5,0.75,29.5,1,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,
(0~1.25~Rail-1|0~28.375~Rail-2),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1
The Mid-Rail has no intersections so its record would simple be void of the variable:
MidRail-1,2.5,0.75,17.875,1,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1, ,ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1
The Mullion would be defined much like the Right Stile:
Mullion-1,2.5,0.75,24.5,1,JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,
(0~10.5~ MidRail -1|0~15.5~ MidRail -2) ,ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1
The Top and Bottom Rails in this case would be identical with the exception of machining side orientation:
Rail-1,2.5,0.75,52.5,1, JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,( 1~20.375~Mullion-1),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1
Rail-2,2.5,0.75,52.5,1, JOB,Red Oak,Unit1,1,0, Profile1,( 0~20.375~Mullion-1),ProfS0,ProfS1,ProfE0,ProfE1

Biesse Door Machine

Output for Biesse Door Machine. Output is one program per job. Doors require additional parameters for proper output: _BD_BIESSEDOOR, _BD_HINGETYPE, _BD_PROFILES, _BD_TOPHCUP, _BD_BOTHCUP,
_BD_PULLPOSX, _BD_PULLPOSY, _BD_PULLDESC, _BD_DOORNOTES

Sample Output

       [DISTINTA : CAMPI]
       campoD0=LABL,A,4,4,NULL,0,0
       campoD1=PROG,A,256,11,NULL,0,2
       campoD2=QNTA,U,4,4,NULL,0,0
       campoD3=CONT,U,4,4,NULL,0,0
       campoD4=COMM,A,256,20,NULL,0,0
       [DISTINTA : RIGHE]
       ,ASSEMBLEDDOOR.BPP,1,0,LPX=1200.00 LPY=400.00 PROFILE=EDGE1 TOPHCUP=60 BOTHCUP=65 HINGETYPE=HETTICH HANDLET=3 HANDLEPOSX=102.46 HANDLEPOSY=102.46 SPECIES=CHERRY
       ARCH=2,
       ,00000000PANEL.BPP,1,0,LPX=1200.00 LPY=400.00 PROFILE=EDGE1 SPECIES=MAPLE,
       ,00ARCHTOPRAIL.BPP,1,0,LPX=1200.00 LPY=400.00 PROFILE=EDGE1 SPECIES=MAPLE TYPEARCH=2,
       ,,,,,
       ,,,,,

___________________________________________________________________________________________________________

This part of the topic is a description of the above:

Line beginning with AssembledDoor.bpp,1,0 (is the Name of the assembled Door but has to have the .bpp,1,0)

Followed by the finished size of the door and the profile name for the outside edge, followed by the centerline of each hingecup assembly and hinge name, then handle/knob type/name and X Y location of center line of handle, then
material name. All of descriptions below per the sample above are separated by spaces.

Assembled door operation

     Size & profile outside edges (same tool)
     Sand profile
     Drill for hinges (custom locations for base doors)
     Drill for door pulls
     Drill for bumper pads (not necessary)

The next line contains the Panel Details.

Door panel operation (before assembly)

     Size panel edges
     Profile outside edges (multiple passes depending on wood species)
     Sand profile (multiple passes depending on wood species)

The last line contains the Top Rail Shape which is not currently supported by the S2M Center.

Alphacam Nest

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
Use this output when using Alphacam to nest parts on a sheet. Alphacam supports tool compensation for routers and saws. The following information from the Tool Catalog can not be used by Alphacam and is therefore ignored:
Descent Rate and Maximum Depth Per Pass. Layer names from the Tool Catalog are ignored and will be generated automatically to the default settings:

   1. Panel Dimensions: PL + Part Thickness
   2. Routing: RT_Z + Depth + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Diameter + _F + Feed Rate + Tool Comp
   3. Grooving Operations: SW_Z + Depth + _T + Tool Number + _D + Kerf + _F + Feed Rate + Tool Comp
   4. Vertical Boring: VD_Z + Depth + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Diameter
   5. Horizontal Boring On Left: HL_Z + Z Position + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Size
   6. Horizontal Boring On Right: HR_Z + Z Position + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Size
   7. Horizontal Boring On Top: HT_Z + Z Position + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Size
   8. Horizontal Boring On Bottom: HB_Z + Z Position + _T + Tool Number + _D + Tool Size
Tool selection for routing is made by Tool Number. Be sure to have accurate tool numbers for all Flat and Shaped Router Bits in the Tool Catalog.

Auto Link

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
AutoLink supports tool compensation for routers, Tool Numbers, Feed Rate, Descent Rate & Maximum Depth Per Pass.
Saws should be set to output Center Line paths. One Saw may be setup with multiple kerf sizes. If a tool exists in the Tool Catalog with a kerf that matches the dado width the Planit CNC Center will output a single polyline with
width. The kerf in AutoLink's configuration setup will determine the number of passes the saw takes to create the dado width.
Tool selection, for all tools, is made by Tool Number. If the Tool Number is 0, then AutoLink will attempt to do a tool match automatically.
Each Tool is interrogated for it's maximum depth for a single pass. If an operation exceeds the maximum depth per pass, multiple passes will be generated automatically.
Routes are output different for Open and Closed Paths. Open paths are output as line segments. Closed paths are output as polylines. This enables a feature to be used with AutoLink where it splits the distance of the 1st line in a
polyline and starts the cut here. This eliminates a common problem of overshot in the corners. Closed Path Route Output adds _RI_RO to the layer name to communicate to AutoLink to synthesize the Lead In / Out and Ramp In / Out.
A part description and dimension has been added to a description layer. This description will show next to the file name on the controller for easy identification of the part. The batch.lst is generated so that AutoLink can process the
files automatically.
Layer Name & Pen Number from the Tool Catalog will be ignored. The DXF files created for AutoLink have the following characteristics:

   1. Border: Border + Part Thickness + LL orientation
   2. Description: Stores part information. This information will appear in the comment section of Winner90 and Genesis
   3. Vertical Boring: Vbore + Depth + Tool Number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   4. Horizontal Boring: Hbore + Z position + Tool number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   5. Grooving: Groove + Depth + Tool Number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   6. Routing: Route + Depth + Tool number (If not 0) + Tool compensation + Feed Rate (If not 0) + Descent Rate (If not 0) + Multiple passes + Ramp In/Out (For closed path routing)
Each unique layer gets a consecutive pen number.
All entities are output at 0z with No Thickness.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 159 of 365

A _Mn (where n is the number of passes) is added to the layer name automatically to process the instruction with multiple passes.
Parts are automatically output in the Top Left quadrant + X , +Y to match the interface in AutoLink.
Dimensions in the layer name use p for a decimal place. They output a maximum of 2 places in the layer name for metric and 5 places for imperial .
Horizontal boring is created with 3 line segments open to the edge of the part the hole will be generated on. Warning messages will be produced for horizontal boring that is not on the edge of part.
Tool Number, Feed Rate, Descent Rate & Tool Compensation from the Tool Catalog - will only be used in the layer name if they are not 0.

Biesse Rover

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
On the NC4x x only, starting from the release 4.24, it is possible to add more information to the DXF. These DXF files will support tool compensation for routers and selection of routers by tool names. This output does not support tool
compensation for saws. All saw tools should be set to output Center Line paths. The following information from the Tool Catalog can not be used in this output and are therefore ignored: Tool Numbers, Feed Rate, Descent Rate,
Maximum Depth Per Pass. Layer names will be ignored for all tools except routers. Router tools should have a layer name that matches the tool name on the machine. The following are the standard layer names:

   1. Panel dimensions: PANEL + Part Thickness
   2. Vertical drills: VERTICAL + Depth
   3. Horizontal drills on left side: SIDEA + Z Position
   4. Horizontal drills on front side: SIDEB + Z Position
   5. Horizontal drills on right side: SIDEC + Z Position
   6. Horizontal drills on back side: SIDED + Z Position
   7. Grooving operations: BLADE + Depth
   8. Generic routing operations: Layer Name + Depth + Tool Comp + Lead In/Out
Tool selection, for all tools but routers, is made by a size match between the tool and the operation. Lead In / Out will be added based on tool compensation and if the router operation is on the interior or exterior of the part.

Building Blocks

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
The ASCII output for Building Blocks follows the AutoSoftware version 2.0 file format. Tool compensation for router paths are supported. All saws should be set to output Center Line paths. Tool Number, Feed Rate, Layer Name &
Pen Number from the Tool Catalog will be ignored. Tool selection is made by a diameter match between the tool and the operation. Since some tools are selected by matching the width of the tool and the operation, the output unit
should match that of your machines tool catalog. This is typically metric.

Building Blocks Nest

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
The ASCII output for Building Blocks follows the AutoSoftware version 2.0 file format. Tool compensation for router paths are supported. All saws should be set to output Center Line paths. Tool Number, Feed Rate, Layer Name &
Pen Number from the Tool Catalog will be ignored. Tool selection is made by a diameter match between the tool and the operation. Since some tools are selected by matching the width of the tool and the operation, the output unit

CAD Code

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 160 of 365

CADCode does not support tool compensation for saws. All saw tools should be set to output Center Line paths. CADCode does support tool compensation for routers and recommends setting routers to output with tool compensation.
The following information from the Tool Catalog can not be used by CADCode and is therefore ignored: Descent Rate and Maximum Depth Per Pass. Layer names will be generated automatically to the following settings:

   1. Panel Dimensions: PANEL

   2. Routing: ROUTE_T + Tool Number + F + Feed Rate + Tool Comp

   3. Grooving Operations: GROOVE_T + Tool Number (If not 0)

   4. Vertical Boring: VBORE_T + Tool Number (If Not 0)

   5. Horizontal Boring: HBORE_T + Tool Number (If Not 0)

Routers and Saws are selected by the Tool Number in the layer name. Be sure to have accurate tool numbers for all routers and saws in the Tool Catalog. If the Tool Number for a drill is 0, tool selection is made be a size match with a
tool Diameter. You can call any tool by number in CADCode, including drills. However, there are several reasons to use Diameter rather than number, but there are occasions where you may want a specific drill number or sequence. In
a layered file, each unique drill would require a separate layer, i.e. "VBORE_T12." Since you are specifying a specific tool CADCode cannot optimize the sequence or multiple drops and this tool is therefore removed from the
optimization process. One reason you might wish a specific tool would be if you have a 5mm through bit or countersink that you want instead of a dowel bit. Tool depth has been eliminated from the layer name since CADCode reads
the information directly from the 3D position of each entity. Additional description layers have been added for labeling capabilities.

CADCode Nest

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.

CADCode does not support tool compensation for saws. All saw tools should be set to output Center Line paths. CADCode does support tool compensation for routers and recommends setting routers to output with tool compensation.
The following information from the Tool Catalog can not be used by CADCode and is therefore ignored: Descent Rate and Maximum Depth Per Pass. Layer names will be generated automatically to the following settings:

   1. Panel Dimensions: PANEL

   2. Routing: ROUTE_T + Tool Number + F + Feed Rate + Tool Comp

   3. Grooving Operations: GROOVE_T + Tool Number (If not 0)

   4. Vertical Boring: VBORE_T + Tool Number (If Not 0)

   5. Horizontal Boring: HBORE_T + Tool Number (If Not 0)

Routers and Saws are selected by the Tool Number in the layer name. Be sure to have accurate tool numbers for all routers and saws in the Tool Catalog. If the Tool Number for a drill is 0, tool selection is made be a size match with a
tool Diameter. You can call any tool by number in CADCode, including drills. However, there are several reasons to use Diameter rather than number, but there are occasions where you may want a specific drill number or sequence. In
a layered file, each unique drill would require a separate layer, i.e. "VBORE_T12." Since you are specifying a specific tool CADCode cannot optimize the sequence or multiple drops and this tool is therefore removed from the
optimization process. One reason you might wish a specific tool would be if you have a 5mm through bit or countersink that you want instead of a dowel bit. Tool depth has been eliminated from the layer name since CADCode reads
the information directly from the 3D position of each entity. Additional description layers have been added for labeling capabilities.

Cam 2

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.

Biesse Cam2 DXF files do not support tool compensation. All router and saw tools should be set to output Center Line paths. The following information from the Tool Catalog can not be used by Cam2 and are therefore ignored: Tool
Numbers, Tool Compensation, Feed Rate, Descent Rate, Maximum Depth Per Pass. Layer names will be generated automatically to the default settings:

   1. Panel dimensions: PANEL + Depth

   2. Vertical drills: VERTICAL + Depth

   3. Horizontal drills on left side: SIDEA + Z Position

   4. Horizontal drills on front side: SIDEB + Z Position

   5. Horizontal drills on right side: SIDEC + Z Position

   6. Horizontal drills on back side: SIDED + Z Position

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 161 of 365

   7. Grooving operations: BLADE + Depth
   8. Generic routing operations: ROUTER + Depth
Tool selection is made by a size match between the tool and the operation. To differentiate between multiple routers bits that have the same diameter, enter slightly different tool diameters for each tool and have those diameters match
the tool setup in Cam2.

Speedy Link

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
SpeedyLink supports Tool Compensation for routers, Tool Numbers, Feed Rate, Descent Rate & Maximum Depth Per Pass.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 163 of 365

Saws should be set to output Center Line paths. One saw may be setup with multiple kerf sizes. If a tool exists in the Tool Catalog with a kerf that matches the dado width the Planit CNC Center will output a single polyline with width.
The kerf in SpeedyLink's configuration setup will determine the number of passes the saw takes to create the dado width.
Tool selection, for all tools, is made by Tool Number. If the Tool Number is 0, then SpeedyLink will try to do a tool match automatically.
Each Tool is interrogated for it's Maximum Depth for a single pass. If an operation exceeds the Maximum Depth Per Pass, multiple passes will be generated automatically.
Routes are output different for Open and Closed Paths. Open paths are output as line segments. Closed paths are output as polylines. This enables a feature to be used with SpeedyLink where it splits the distance of the 1st line in a
polyline and starts the cut here. This eliminates a common problem of overshot in the corners. Closed Path Route Output adds _RI_RO to the layer name to communicate to SpeedyLink to synthesize the Lead In / Out and Ramp In /
Out.
A part description and dimension has been added to a description layer. This description will show next to the file name on the controller for easy identification of the part. The batch.lst is generated so that SpeedyLink can process the
files automatically.
Layer Name & Pen Number from the Tool Catalog will be ignored. The DXF files created for SpeedyLink have the following characteristics:

   1. Border: Border + Part Thickness + LL orientation
   2. Description: Stores part information
   3. Vertical Boring: Vbore + Depth + Tool Number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   4. Horizontal Boring: Hbore + Z position + Tool number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   5. Grooving: Groove + Depth + Tool Number (If not 0) + Multiple passes
   6. Routing: Route + Depth + Tool number (If not 0) + Tool compensation + Feed Rate (If not 0) + Descent Rate (If not 0) + Multiple passes + Ramp In/Out (For closed path routing)
Each unique layer gets a consecutive pen number.
All entities are output at 0z with No Thickness.
A _Mn (where n is the number of passes) is added to the layer name automatically to process the instruction with multiple passes.
Parts are automatically output in the Top Left quadrant + X , +Y to match the interface in SpeedyLink.
Dimensions in the layer name use p for a decimal place. They output a maximum of 2 places in the layer name for metric and 5 places for imperial .
Horizontal boring is created with 3 line segments open to the edge of the part the hole will be generated on. Warning messages will be produced for horizontal boring that is not on the edge of part.
Tool Number, Feed Rate, Descent Rate & Tool Compensation from the Tool Catalog - will only be used in the layer name if they are not 0.

TASK

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 172 of 365

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
The TASK output is an ASCII file format designed with the AutoSoftware 2.0 file specifications. This format only supports Center Line paths for saw. Depending on the post-processor, Feed Rate, Tool Number & Tool Compensation
may be ignored. Valid Feed Rate values are 0 - 9. Layer Name & Pen Number from the Tool Catalog are always ignored. Tool selection for boring is made by a diameter match in the tool and operation. Tool selection for saws &
routers are made by either Tool Number or Tool Diameter depending on the post-processor. Since some tools are selected by matching the width of the tool and the operation, the output unit should match that of your machines tool
catalog. This is typically metric.

TASK Version 3

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
TASK version 3 and earlier do not support Tool Compensation or Tool Number. All saws and routers should be set to output Center Line paths. Feed Rate, Tool Number, Tool Compensation, Layer Name & Pen Number from the Tool
Catalog will be ignored. Tool selection is made by a diameter match between the tool and the operation. Since some tools are selected by matching the width of the tool and the operation, the output unit should match that of your
machines tool catalog. This is typically metric.

TASK Version 5

This is a Parts Router and/or Point-to-Point Link.
TASK version 5 supports both Tool Compensation for routers and Tool Numbers for saws and routers. All saws should be set to output Center Line paths. Feed Rate, Layer Name & Pen Number from the Tool Catalog will be ignored.
Tool selection will be made by Tool Number. If the Tool Number is 0, then tool selection is made by a diameter match between the tool and the operation. Since some tools are selected by matching the width of the tool and the
operation, the output unit should match that of your machines tool catalog. This is typically metric.

Setting Up Real Time Labels for Holz-Her Saws

See the Machine Catalog Saw topic for Label Setup information.

Real time Label Printing Using a S2M Center CPOUT file and a Label picture

Basic setup

To implement this on a CUT 85 saw the design software needs to produce 2 items. These items are a CPOUT file and label pictures. The CPOUT files should be placed in the C:\CPOutPfad folder, and the label pictures in the
C:\CV_Pictures folder. Make these folders if they do not exist. Next check that the saw is set to look at the CPOUT folder as in the example below (figure 1). The folders can have any name you want, you just need to enter the proper
names in the proper places.

       Figure 1 - CPOUT file location

Label design

The next item to do is to design the label file at the saw. Set the label size by selecting "F5 Size". Enter the label width and length to match the S2M Center Label Designer label size. In "Line definition" set "Type" to "Variable
picture". Set "X-Pos" and "Y-Pos" to "0.000" and width and height to match the label size. The next item "Variable:" needs to match the location of the picture file name in the CPOUT file. Use "14 Element identifier" if the value is in
the "PRT1 partno" field. Use "62 Element info 1" if the value is in the "PRT1 drillpat" field. Set "Format:" to 8 to strip out any extra characters. Last set "Path:" to your selected picture folder. Also add "#.XXX" with the XXX being
your picture file format (*.bmp, *.ico, *.jpg, *.jpeg, *.emf, *.wmf). Below is the screen shot of the label design screen in the CUT 85 software (Figure 2).

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 175 of 365

       Figure 2 - Label Format

Select label type

Select "F2 Label type" and double click on "Raw cut label" from the selection box (figure 3). Save the label by selecting "F3 Label..." and select "Save as...". Type a file name that describes the label type and select "OK" (Figure 4)

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 176 of 365
                                                              9/25/2026
         Figure 3 Label type

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                Page 177 of 365

       Figure 4 Save label

Printer Selection

Now that the label style is set and saved, select "F7 Printer" and select the proper printer for your labels (Figure 5). Once the printer is selected Select "F3 Label..." and this time select "Save"

Casadei CN04

Casadei MX39

This is a Panel Saw Link.
This link is for the Casadei MX39 using the NPS400 Controller.

CPOUT

This is a Panel Saw Link.

This link creates a standard CPOUT file with additional formatting conventions as outlined by CADCode. Following is the file specification:

Pattern Export File Specification (CPOUT)

The first three lines describe the header information as follows:

The HDR1 line contains the cutlist number, Job name and Materials in the following format:

HDR1,cnum,jobmat

HDR1              is the line descriptor

cnum              indicates the cutlist number, a string with leading zeros included.

jobmat            indicates the cutlist title, an ASCII string of length 50 or less, composed of the Material Name and Job Information.

The HDR2 line contains information regarding the date and time the cutlist was optimized in the following format:

HDR2,date,time

HDR2              is the line descriptor

date              indicates the date of optimization. It is an ASCII string of length 12. [Dec 08, 2006]

time              indicates the time of optimization. It is an ASCII string of length 8. [01:12 PM]

The HDR3 line contains information about the product that produced the pattern data file in the following format:

HDR3,bnum,ver,pcode,sawid

HDR3              is the line descriptor

bnum              indicates the Build number of the product used. It is an ASCII string of length 6.

ver               CPOUT Version [2.01]

pcode             indicates product code [CVI]

sawid             indicates the SawLink ID used [0009]

The next three lines describe the controls used in the pattern making process.

The CTL1 line contains the dimensional unit of the cutlist, the output unit, the grain indicator, area reporting, and saw type in the following format:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                      Page 180 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                     9/25/2026
CTL1,unit1,unit2,grain,areafac,sawtype

CTL1                is the line descriptor.

unit1               indicates dimensional Unit of Measure. It is a one character field as follows:

                    F = Fractional Units

                    D = Decimal Inches

                    M = Millimeters

unit2               indicates cutting instruction Unit of Measure. It is a one character field as described for unit1 and also always matches unit1.

grain               indicates the graining control. It is a one character field as follows:

                    Y = Cutlist is grained

                    N = Cutlist is not grained

areafac             indicates the area unit throughout the file. *Planit always outputs 1. 1 = SqFT in Imperial and SqM in Mtric based on the unit1 setting.

sawtype indicates the type of saw specified. It is a one character field as follows:

                    0 = no saw type specified

                    1 = single axis saw

                    2 = two axis saw with single feeder

                    3 = two axis saw with multiple feeder

The CTL2 line describes dimensional content in the following format:

CTL2,rkerf,xkerf,maxsw,minsw,pwadj,pladj

CTL2                is the line descriptor

rkerf               indicates the rip cut kerf

xkerf               indicates the cross cut kerf

maxsw               indicates the maximum strip section width

minsw               indicates the minimum strip section width

pwadj       indicates the part width adjustment. *Planit always outputs 0.

pladj               indicates the part length adjustment. *Planit always outputs 0.

The CTL3 line describes the Front Trim value in the following format:

CTL3,ftrim

CTL3                is the line descriptor

ftrim               indicates the value for the Front Trim.

The THK1 line specifies the sheet stock thickness and stack (book) height in the following format:

THK1,sthick,stackh

THK1                is the line descriptor

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                       Page 181 of 365

sthick indicates the sheet stock thickness

stackh    indicates the maximum stack height. This is a measurement not the number of sheets.

The next two lines describe cutting statistics.

The STA1 line specifies the total number of cuts, grouped by each phase type, as indicated below. Each field is right justified and has a length of 6 digits, and is in the following format:

STA1,nrcut,nxcut,n3cut

STA1      is the line descriptor

nrcut     indicates the number of rip cuts in the cutlist and includes the leading trim cut in the calculation.

nxcut     indicates the number of cross cuts in the cutlist and includes the trim cut. If there are identical strips then the cross cuts are not counted on the additional strips.

n3cut     indicates the number of third or greater phase cuts in the cut list.

The STA2 line specifies the total distances cut, grouped by each phase type. The distances are a function of the dimensional units (unit1). If the dimensional units are fractional or decimal inches, the distances are
in feet, If the dimensional units are millimeters, the distances are in meters. Each field is right justified to eleven characters and has the form `xxxxxxx.yyy'.

The STA2 line has the following format:

STA2,drcut,dxcut,d3cut

STA2      is the line descriptor

drcut     indicates the total distance of rip cuts in the cutlist including the leading trim cut.

dxcut     indicates the total distance of cross cuts in the cutlist including the trim cut. Unlike the nxcut field in STA1, the cross cut distance does not skip identical strips.

d3cut     indicates the total distance of third or greater phase cuts in the cutlist.

The next three lines describe sheet stock records.

The INV1 record for each sheet stock item has the following format:

INV1,supply,used,ucost,width,length,trim1,trim2,trim3,trim4

INV1      is the line descriptor

supply    indicates the available supply of the sheet. It is a right justified numeric field of length 5. A supply of `*****' indicates an infinite supply.

used      indicates the quantity of the sheet used in the cutlist. It is aright justified numeric field of length 5.

ucost     indicates the unit cost per square foot/meter. It is a right justified field of length 9 in the form `xxx.yyyy'.

width     indicates the width of the sheet.

length    indicates the length of the sheet.

trim1     indicates the leading width trim.

trim2     indicates the leading length trim.

trim3     indicates the trailing width trim.

trim4     indicates the trailing length trim.

The INV2 record for each sheet stock item has the following format:

INV2,desc,areacut,finparts,lpfin

INV2      is the line descriptor

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                          9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                    Page 182 of 365
                                                                                                                                   9/25/2026
desc      indicates the description of the sheet. It is a character field of length 24.

areacut   indicates the total area cut of the sheet. [xxxxxxxxx.yyyy]

finparts  indicates the total area finished (parts in sheet). [xxxxxxxxx.yyyy]

lpfin     indicates the total low priority area finished. *Planit always outputs 0.

The INV3 record is used to describe extra information about the sheet stock and has the following format:

INV3,vendor,location,ori

INV3      is the line descriptor

vendor    indicates the vendor from which the sheet stock was purchased. *Planit does not use this field.

location indicates the physical location in the shop for the sheet stock. *Planit does not use this field.

ori       is the orientation that the user prefers for the first cut on the sheet. It is a one character field as follows:

                          0 = don't care

                          1 = first cut along length

                          2 = first cut along width

The next two lines describe part cut sizes.

The ORD1 record for each cut size has the following format:

ORD1,demand,supply,width,length,prio,grain

ORD1      is the line descriptor

demand indicates the quantity requested for the cut size. It is a right justified field of length 5.

supply    indicates the quantity supplied/produced of the cut size. It is a right justified field of length 5.

width     indicates the width.

length    indicates the length.

prio      indicates the priority of the part. It is a one character field where:

                          H or Blank indicates high priority (NcCenter always outputs an H

                          L indicates low priority

grain     *Planit leaves blank because it is indicated in CTL1.

The ORD2 record for each cut size has the following format:

ORD2,pcost,desc1,desc2

ORD2      is the line descriptor

pcost     cost per part to 1/10 of a cent. [dddd.ccc]

desc1     indicates the primary part description. *Planit leaves blank because it is described in PRT

desc2     indicates the secondary part description. *Planit leaves blank because it is described in PRT

The ORD3 record is used to access the part information for each cut size and has the following format:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                 Page 183 of 365
                                                                                                                9/25/2026
ORD3,nparts,index

ORD3               is the line descriptor

nparts             This is the number of parts that have been combined into this cut size.

index              This is the index into the list of parts where this cut size begins.

The next five records are for the parts that make up the above cut sizes.

The PRT1 record for each part has the following format:

PRT1,dmnd,ordno,width,length,dolabel,prtname,jobname,partcom,

cncprg

PRT1               is the line descriptor

dmnd               is the quantity the user specified for this part

ordno              is the index or offset into the cut sizes specified above

width              is the width for this part

length             is the length for this part

dolabel            indicates if user wants a label made for this Part. *Planit always outputs 1 (True).

prtname is the name of this part

jobname is the name of the Job this part is in

partcom is the name of the Label Graphic for Real-Time Label printing

cncprg             CNC Program Name - 07/06/06(date) - Primary Barcode

The PRT2 record for each part has the following format:

PRT2,postlam,secprog,roomname

PRT2               is the line descriptor

postlam indicates post laminating instructions. *Planit always outputs 0.

secprog is the Secondary CNC Program Name

rmname is the name of the Room the part is in

The PRT3 record for each part has the following format:

PRT3,edges,priprog,wmatl1,wcolor1,prizone,lmatl1,lcolor1,secprog,wmatl2,wcolor2,seczone,lmatl2,lcolor2

PRT3               is the line descriptor

edges              is the edge banding codes for the Part [W1,L1,W2,L2]

                   0 = No Banding

                   1 = Door Banding

                   2 = Exterior Banding

                   3 = Interior Banding

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                              Page 184 of 365
                                                                                                                             9/25/2026
          4 = BandingB
          5 = BandingC
          6 = BandingF
          7 = BandingG
          8 = BandingH
          9 = BandingJ

                             Other Banding letters are ignored as CPOUT only has placeholders for 0 - 9 identifiers.

priprog   is the Primary CNC Program Name

wmatl1    is the Edge Band Material for W1

wcolor1 is the Edge Band Finish Color for W1

prizone   is the Primary CNC Program Zone

lmatl1 is the Edge Band Material Name for L1

lcolor1   is the Edge Band Finish Color for L1

secprog is the Secondary CNC Program Name

wmatl2    is the Edge Band Material for W2

wcolor2 is the Edge Band Finish Color for W2

seczone is the Secondary CNC Program Zone

lmatl2 is the Edge Band Material for L2

lcolor2   is the Edge Band Finish Color for L2

The PRT4 record for each part has the following format:

PRT4,dname,cwidth,cheight,cdepth,ddesc,comments

PRT4      is the line descriptor

cname     is the name of the Cabinet this part is in

cwidth    is the width of the Cabinet this part is in

cheight   is the height of the Cabinet this part is in

cdepth    is the depth of the Cabinet this part is in

cdesc     is the description of the Cabinet this part is in

comments are the comments on the Cabinet this part is in

The PRT5 record for each part has the following format:

PRT5,prio,grain,qty,qtyover

PRT5      is the line descriptor

prio      is the priority which is always set to high (H)

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       Page 185 of 365

grain     indicates grain [Y or N] - Blank indicates use the global setting as defined in CTL1

qty       this parts quantity

qtyover   this parts quantity plus overmake

Following the part records are the drop records. There is one record for each drop. The number of the drop items what will appear in the file is specified in the second line of the file as described above.

The format of the drop record is as follows:

OFF1,supply,width,length

OFF1      is the line descriptor

supply    is the quantity of the drop

width     is the width of the drop

length    is the length of the drop

Following the drop records are the pattern description records.

For each pattern in the file there are five header records. Following the header records are the cut type records.

The five pattern records are as follows:

The first pattern record has the following format:

PAT1,invn,pat_type,repeat,cycles

PAT1      is the line descriptor

invn      indicates the sequence number of the sheet stock item used to cut the pattern, It is a string length of 2. with the leading zeros included.

pat_type indicates a one character field indicating the type of pattern as follows:

                          L = Long rip pattern

                          S = Short rip pattern

                          H = Head cut pattern

repeat    indicates the number of sheets in the book (stack)

cycles     indicates the number of cycles (books/stacks cut). i.e. If you have 11 sheets, but the book height is 3 sheets, the cycle would be 4 indicating (3 + 3 + 3 + 2). With the NcCenter's current output
          the cycle is always 1 because the NcCenter pre-groups the materials by maximum book (stack) height.

       NOTE: A long rip pattern indicates that the rip cuts are made parallel to the larger dimension of the sheet. i.e. If the width dimension entered is less than the length dimension entered for the sheet, this indicates that the rip cuts are parallel to the length dimension of the sheet, But, if the width
       dimension is larger that the length dimension, the rip cuts are made parallel to the width dimension entered.

       A short rip pattern indicates that the rip cuts are made parallel to the shorter dimension of the sheet. i.e. If the width dimension entered is less than the length dimension for the sheet, this indicates that the rip cuts are made parallel to the width of the sheet. But, if the width dimension is larger that the
       length dimension, the rip cuts are made parallel to the length dimension.

       A head cut pattern has the first cut across the width of the sheet (like a short rip) but after the initial head cut the rest of the pattern looks like a long rip. The strips within the head section are identical. The size of the head section is controlled by the cutting rules.

The second pattern header record has the following format:

PAT2,yield,uarea,uareah,uareal,cpua

PAT2      is the line descriptor

yield     indicates the percentage of yield

uarea     indicates the used area per panel

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                 Page 186 of 365

      uareah  indicates the used area by high priority parts. *All Planit Parts are high priority so this field always matches the value in uarea.

      uareal  indicates the used area by low priority parts. *All Planit parts are high priority so this field is always 0.

      cpua    indicates the cost of the used area per panel

The third pattern header record has the format below. Each field is right justified to a length of 5.

PAT3,nrcut,nxcut,n3cut,mnxcut

      PAT3    is the line descriptor

      nrcut   indicates the number of rip cuts per cycle

      nxcut   indicates the number of cross cuts per cycle

      n3cut   indicates the number of 3rd and higher phase cuts per cycle

      mnxcut  indicates the number of cross cuts in the worst strip in the pattern

The fourth pattern header record has the format below. Each field in the record is right justified and is 11 characters in length with the format `XXXXXXX.YYY'.

PAT4,drcut,dxcut,d3cut

      PAT4    is the line descriptor

      drcut   indicates the total rip cut distance per cycle

      dxcut   indicates the total cross cut distance per cycle

      d3cut   indicates the total of 3rd and higher phase cuts per cycle

The fifth pattern header record has the following format:

PAT5,ton,toff,con,coff

      PAT5    is the line descriptor

      ton     indicates the on-line time in minutes for all cycles

      toff    indicates the off-line time in minutes for all panels

      con     indicates the on-line operating cost

      coff    indicates the off-line operating cost

Following the PAT5 record will be one of the following record types.

Patterns that have head cuts are divided into two sections. We call these sections the "head section" and the "body section". The cutting instructions (the CUT1 records) do not change for the head section, they
still have codes for rip cuts, cross cuts, and 3rd and higher phase cuts. The head section will be preceded by a record that starts with the keyword "HCUT", followed by the CUT1 records. At the end of the head
section (or at the start of the body section) you will have a record that starts with the keyword "BCUT", followed by the CUT1 records for this part of the pattern.

HCUT

      The presence of this record means that this pattern has a head section and the CUT1 records for the head section will follow this record.

      Note that this record is OPTIONAL and will NOT appear for patterns that do not have head cuts.

BCUT

      This record will precede the CUT1 records for the pattern. If the pattern contains head cuts then this record will precede the CUT1 records for the body section.

Following the above HCUT or BCUT records are pattern description records, which indicate how to cut the sheet. This record has the following format:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                    9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                   Page 187 of 365

CUT1,level,dist,otype,onum

CUT1      this is the line descriptor

level     indicates the phase number of the cut. It is a two character field with leading zeros included, i.e.,

                            01 = rip cut

                            02 = cross cut

                            03 = 3rd phase cut

                            04 = 4th phase cut, etc.

dist      indicates the dimensional distance of the cut

otype     indicates the part type. It is a one character field where:

                            0 = Part not produced with cut

                            1 = Scheduled part

                            2 = Drop part

onum       indicates the sequence number of the size produced with this cut. If additional cuts are required before a cut size is produced with a cut, a zero is supplied for onum. Thus a value of `5' means
          that this cut results in a finished cut size whose index is `5' in the ORD records (the first ORD record is `1').

NOTE: Instructions are not provided for leading trim cuts. They must be determined from the corresponding sheet stock item and the head cut trim setting.

The following indicates the end of the file:
END

Based on CPOUT specification by Pattern Systems International. Revised to meet CADCode specifications. Revised to meet Planit NcCenter specifications.

File Properties Area

There are ten general options available to configure your output:

File Name - This field will allow you to specify a specific or partial file name. You may choose to use {JobName} as a variable. Any use of {JobName} will be replaced by the current job.
       Example settings:
               {JobName}.txt  Smith.txt
               MyOutput.csv  MyOutput.csv
               Out-{JobName}.lst  Out-Smith.lst

Header - The header is optional. If specified it will be placed once at the top of the file. It can be left blank to omit. Any use of \n will be replaced with a carriage return.
       Example Setting: ;This is a heading\nThis is the second line\nThis is the 3rd line.
               ;This is a heading
               This is the second line
               This is the 3rd line.

Fixed Width - This will determine if each field will use a specific number of characters in the output. If the actual data has fewer characters then that specified the remaining characters will be padded with space. If the actual data
exceeds the character limit then the value will be truncated.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm              9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                               Page 189 of 365

              String Length includes the Text Qualifier. If the Field Length is set to 4 and the String is "ABCDEFG" the output will be "AB". If there is no text qualifier the output would be ABCD.
To set the length of the output field first select the field on the right then enter the Fixed Length at the bottom of the list.

              If Fixed Width is false then the Text Length indicates the maximum length of any field. This by default can be left at zero, but can be set to a number it you wish to limit the output length. Unlike Fixed Width, the max
       length does not include the text qualifier. A max length of 4 with the string ABCDEF would result "ABCD"
Delimiter - This is an optional single character to separate each field. It is commonly a comma (,).
Text Qualifier - This is an optional single character that will surround and text or memo output. Typical values are (") and (`).

              When the output type is not fixed width if the text qualifier character is found in a text or memo field it will be replaced with either " or ` (whichever is NOT the text qualifier character). The example below uses a double
       quote (") as the text qualifier.
       A "sample" string with "double" and `single' quotes: "A `sample' string with `double' and `single' quotes."
Units - The unit can either be Inches or MM. As an option the unit may also be multiplied by a factor of 10, 100, or 1000.

Units Precision - This option specifies the unit precision. Value will be rounded to the nearest selection. The unit precision applies to measurements as well as singles and doubles.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                               Page 190 of 365

Boolean Format - This option allows for multiple Boolean formats.

Date Format - A string value using (M)onth, (D)ay & (Y)ear as variables. Example settings:

M/D/YY                 10/4/13

DD-MM-YYYY             04-10-2013

DDD DD-MM-YYYY         Mon 04-10-2013

DDD, MMM DD/YY         Mon, Oct 04/13

DDDD, MMMM DD of YYYY Monday, October 04 of 2013

Currency Precision - Similar to unit precision, the value will be rounded to the specified precision.

                                                                                                             PATTERN EXCHANGE FILE SPECIFICATION
                                                                                                                                         V1.02.4

                                                                                                                            Bath Software Research Ltd
                                                                                                                                  04 November 1999

1. INTRODUCTION

This document describes a data structure for the exchange of cutting lists and patterns for sheet material between various design programs, optimising packages, and panel saw controllers.
This data structure contains the information that is required for transferring cutting lists to an optimising package and for transferring optimised cutting patterns with label information to a panel saw. The data structure can be created in
two formats as follows.

     ASCII comma-separated file (Created by the Planit NcCenter)
     Access database
The data structure consists of 12 record types each with a number of fields. In the ASCII file each record type is represented by a token which begins each line and in the database each record type is a separate table.
Each record type and each field name are in uppercase and use underscores between separate words. No table name or field name is more than 10 characters.

2. RECORD TYPES & TABLE NAMES

The `cutting list' record types are as follows.
HEADER - general information describing the complete data structure (or file)
JOBS - header data for each job (cutting list or optimised run)
PARTS_REQ - basic requirement details for each item in the cutting list
PARTS_INF - standard information about each part
PARTS_UDI - user-defined information about each part
BOARDS - information about each item in the board (stock) list
MATERIALS - information about each material type
NOTES - other information for a job
The `post-optimisation' records are:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                           Page 195 of 365

OFFCUTS - record describing each off-cut produced
PATTERNS - pattern header records - one for each cutting pattern
CUTS - cutting instructions - occur many times per pattern - once for each cut required
VECTORS - vector graphics describing the pattern

3. FORMAT

The ASCII version of the file uses standard comma-separated format, and has the suffix .PTX (PaTtern eXchange). The main part of the filename could be the job/order number or batch name if the file contains multiple jobs.

Examples:-

01234.PTX   ABC123-1.PTX

Note that the structure allows for the ASCII file to contain more than 1 cutting list or run if necessary, for example it could contain a batch of runs. Note that there may be restrictions on the file name because some controllers will, for
example, only accept 5 digits for the job number.

All normal CSV format conventions apply, including optional use of quotation marks around text data. Leading spaces are ignored. Trailing commas (separators) are not necessary. Text fields containing commas must be enclosed in
quotes, and commas will be replaced by `blank' (ASCII code 32) on import.

The format and size restrictions for each field are tabulated in section 18. Note, that the limitations (eg. max length of material code) will vary according to the implementation and specification of the saw.

All `index numbers' must be integer values, starting at 1 for the first record, and incrementing consecutively up to the maximum specified. Note, in particular that all part, board, pattern and cutting records must contain the appropriate
job index number showing which job they relate to.

The Access database version stores each record type in a separate table. The file has the standard extension of MDB.

Examples:

01234.MDB   BATCH32.MDB

4. HEADER - GENERAL INFORMATION

HEADER - VERSION, TITLE, UNITS, ORIGIN, TRIM_TYPE
The header record contains descriptive and global information for the job. This record appears as a line in the ASCII file. In the Access database this information will be stored in the database properties.
VERSION - File version (set to1)
TITLE - File title
UNITS - Measurement mode = 0 (metric), 1(decimal inches).
ORIGIN - This field indicates the origin for the VECTOR drawing records. The origin for the CUT records is assumed to be 0 (top left).

       0 = top to bottom - left to right
       1 = top to bottom - right to left
       2 = bottom to top - left to right
       3 = bottom to top - right to left
TRIM_TYPE - Indicates whether the waste strip/piece is cut first or last. That is, is the fixed trim done on the leading edge or as a final trim?
       0 = trim waste piece first

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                              9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                          Page 196 of 365

       1 = trim fixed trim first               TITLE         UNITS                         ORIGIN                                                                                                TRIM_TYPE
ASCII & Database examples                This is an example     0                              0                                                                                                         1
PROPERTIES,1,"This is an example",0,0,1
PROPERTIES TABLE                                                                                                                                                                              DATE
                                                                                                                                                                                            17/01/1999
                     VERSION

                           1

5. JOBS - JOB RECORD

JOBS, - JOB_INDEX, NAME, DESC, ORD_DATE, CUT_DATE, CUSTOMER, STATUS
This record contains data about each job contained in the file. These records are optional and in the absence of job records all parts and patterns are assumed to belong to the same job.
JOB_INDEX - Unique index number used to link other records to an appropriate job
NAME - Job number/name - reference for job
DESC - Job description/title - title of job
ORD_DATE - Date of order (DD/MM/YYYY)
CUT_DATE - Date for cutting/delivery (DD/MM/YYYY)
CUSTOMER - Customer code or name
STATUS - Status of the job.

       0 - not optimised
       1 - optimised
       2 - optimise failed
       Note: there may be a range of other error codes
ASCII & Database examples
JOBS,1,ORD1234,SAMPLE JOB - CUSTOMER WOODCO,17/01/1999, 22/01/1999,WOODCO,0
JOBS TABLE

JOB_INDEX    NAME                                            DESC

          1  ORD1234                                         SAMPLE JOB - CUSTOMER WOODCO

6. PARTS_REQ - PART REQUIREMENT RECORD

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                       9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                           Page 197 of 365

PARTS_REQ - JOB_INDEX, PART_ INDEX, CODE, MAT_INDEX, LENGTH, WIDTH, QTY_REQ, QTY_OVER, QTY_UNDER, GRAIN, QTY_PROD
This record contains data about each different size (or line item) in the cutting list. This record is used to provide details about each part (over and above cut sizes).
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
PART_INDEX - Index number to link this record with other associated part records
CODE - Part code or description.
MAT_INDEX - Index of material used for this part.
LENGTH - Cut length of part shown in appropriate measurement unit
WIDTH - Cut length of part shown in appropriate measurement unit
QTY_REQ - number of pieces this size
QTY_OVER - allowed over production
QTY_UNDER - allowed under production.
GRAIN - 0=No grain/part can be rotated, 1 = grain/part cannot be rotated.
QTY_PROD - quantity of parts produced by patterns

ASCII & Database examples
PARTS_REQ,1,1,SD900X,1,890.0,645.5,50,0,2,0,50
PARTS-REQ TABLE

JOB_INDEX    PART_INDEX                         CODE    MAT_INDEX  LENGTH                                                                                                   WIDTH  .....

          1  1                                  SD900X  1          890.0                                                                                                    645.5  .....

7. PARTS_INF - STANDARD PART INFO RECORD

PARTS_INF - JOB_INDEX, PART_INDEX, DESC, LABEL_QTY, FIN_LENGTH, FIN_WIDTH, ORDER, EDGE1, EDGE2, EDGE3, EDGE4, EDG_PG1, EDG_PG2, EDG_PG3, EDG_PG4, FACE_LAM,
BACK_LAM, CORE_MAT, PALLET, DRAWING, PRODUCT, PROD_INFO, PROD_WIDTH, PROD_HGT, PROD_DEPTH, PROD_NUM, ROOM, BARCODE1, BARCODE2
This optional record contains standard information about each different size (or line item) in the cutting list. One use of this record is to hold data for label printing.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
PART_INDEX - Index number linking this record with other part records.
DESC - A second part description
LABEL_QTY - Number of copies of the label for this part.

       0 = no labels for this part
       default if not specified =1
FIN_LENGTH - Length of part after edging and trimming

EDGE1 - Code or description of edging for bottom (length) edge
EDGE2 - Code or description of edging for top (length) edge
EDGE3 - Code or description of edging for left (width) edge
EDGE4 - Code or description of edging for right (width) edge
EDG_PG1 - Program or operation code for bottom (length) edge
EDG_PG2 - Program or operation code for top (length) edge

FACE_LAM - Code/description of laminate material for face (topside) of part

CORE_MAT - Code or description of core material
PALLET - Pallet layout (stacks in length and width)

PROD_INFO - Description of product or cabinet
PROD_WIDTH - External dimension of product or cabinet
PROD_HGT - External dimension of product or cabinet
PROD_DEPTH - External dimension of product or cabinet
PROD_NUM - Item number of cabinet in room
ROOM - Room or floor or group item number for cabinet

BARCODE2 - data for second bar code - as text string

8. PARTS_UDI - USER DEFINED PART INFO RECORD

PARTS_UDI - JOB_INDEX, PART_INDEX, INFO1, INFO2, .... INFO30
This optional record contains user-defined information about each different size (or line item) in the cutting list. One use of this data is for label printing.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
PART_INDEX - Index number linking this record with other part records
INFOn
There are up to 30 information fields. The name of each field is INFO followed by the field number. The fields may be used for any purpose such as customer details, dates, CNC operations, and other items not included in the other
part records.

JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
BRD_INDEX - index number linking this record with the PATTERNS records for this job.
CODE - Board code - usually the stock code for the sheet size.

QTY_STOCK - Total number of sheets available - default 99999 (0=none)
QTY_USED - Total number of sheets this size used in patterns

10. MATERIALS - MATERIAL RECORDS

MATERIALS - JOB_INDEX, MAT_INDEX, CODE, DESC, THICK, BOOK, KERF_RIP, KERF_XCT, TRIM_FRIP, TRIM_VRIP, TRIM_FXCT, TRIM_VXCT, TRIM_HEAD, TRIM_FRCT, TRIM_VRCT,
RULE1, RULE2, RULE3, RULE4
These records define the different material types. There should be a least one of these records in data structure. This record is used to pass a detailed material description, the thickness and other parameters which may vary according to
material type.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
MAT_INDEX - Unique index of material used to link this record to other records
CODE - Material code
DESC - Material description
THICK - Material thickness in appropriate measurement mode
BOOK - Max sheets per book, reflects cutting height of saw
KERF_RIP - Rip saw kerf - in unit of measurement
KERF_XCT - Crosscut saw kerf - in unit of measurement
TRIM_FRIP - Fixed rip trim (Rear) - includes saw kerf - amount sheet size is reduced by
TRIM_VRIP - Minimum variable rip trim (Front) - includes saw kerf
TRIM_FXCT - Fixed crosscut trim (Rear) - includes saw kerf
TRIM_VXCT - Minimum variable cross cut trim (Front) - includes saw kerf
TRIM_HEAD - Internal head cut trim - includes saw kerf
TRIM_FRCT - Fixed recut trim (Rear) - includes saw kerf
TRIM_VRCT - Minimum variable recut trim (Front) - includes saw kerf
RULE1 - Optimisation rule 1 - cut nesting limit - 1 to 9 (e.g. 3 = allow third phase recuts)
RULE2 - Optimisation rule 2 - head cuts allowed (0=No, 1 =Yes)
RULE3 - Optimisation rule 3 - board rotation allowed (short rip) (0=No, 1=Yes)

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                            9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                        Page 201 of 365

RULE4 - Optimisation rule 4 - show separate patterns for duplicate parts (0=No 1=Yes)

The saw kerf and trims are optional. Note that it is assumed that one of the two rip trims will be constant and the other rip trim includes the waste strip. Either (a) the leading edge is trimmed and the waste strip comes out last, or (b)
the waste strip is removed by the first rip and the last rip is a constant trim. This assumption also applies to cross cut trims and recut trims.

ASCII & Database examples
MAT,1,1,WHITE18,"White laminate -chipboard18mm",18,4,4.8,4.8,10,10,8,8,8,8,8,
4,1,1,1
MATERIALS TABLE

                                                                               White Laminate - chipboard 18mm               18
          1  1                                                 WHITE18                                                                                                       4     .....

11. NOTES - NOTE RECORDS

NOTES - JOB_INDEX, NOTES_INDEX, TEXT
This record is optional and holds any messages or notes that need to be associated with a job, for example customer details, special instructions, etc, or any details that are job related rather than part or material related. As many lines
as required can be stored for each job.
JOB_INDEX - index linking note to job
NOTES_INDEX - index storing order of notes
TEXT - text of note - Maximum length of text field is 250 characters.

JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.

CODE - Offcut code or description - used to identify offcut.
MAT_INDEX - Index of material used for this offcut. Enables offcuts of similar material composition, thickness and colour, but different size to be grouped together.

13. PATTERNS - PATTERN RECORDS

PATTERNS - JOB_INDEX, PTN_INDEX, BRD_INDEX, TYPE, QTY_RUN, CYCLES, MAX _BOOK, PICTURE
This record occurs once per pattern. It is used to describe header detail for the pattern, such as board size used, number of sheets to be cut etc.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
PTN_INDEX - Sequential number incrementing by 1 for each pattern record for each job.
BRD_INDEX - Index number from the Boards records.
TYPE - determines the direction of the first cut, and the type of pattern

       0 = rip length first - non-head cut pattern
       1 = turn board before ripping - non-head cut pattern
       2 = head cut pattern - head cut across width
       3 = head cut pattern - head cut along length
       4 = crosscut only
QTY_RUN - Run quantity - number of sheets to be cut to this pattern
QTY_CYCLES - Number of cycles or books
MAX_BOOK - Maximum number of sheets per book (cutting height)
PICTURE - Name of file containing picture of cutting pattern

Main Tab                                                                 Page 203 of 365

14. CUTS - CUTTING INSTRUCTIONS

CUTS - JOB_INDEX, PTN_INDEX, CUT_INDEX, SEQUENCE, FUNCTION, DIMENSION, QTY_RPT, PART_INDEX, QTY_PART, COMMENT
These records define each cut for the saw and determine the parts produced by each cut. This is used, for example, so that the correct labels can be printed at the saw in synchronisation with the cutting.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job.
PTN_INDEX - Index number used to link this record with pattern records
CUT_INDEX - Sequential index number incrementing by 1 for each cut
record for each job
SEQUENCE - Cut sequence number indicating order in which cuts are processed by saw
FUNCTION - The type of cut:

       0 = head cut
       1 = rip cut
       2 = cross cut
       3 = 3rd phase / recut
       4 = 4th phase /recut
       90,91,92,93 = trim / waste cut corresponding to phase of cut (to override defaults)
DIMENSION - The size of cut in measurement units
QTY_RPT - The repeat quantity for this cut
PART_INDEX - 0 if no part produced or part index number in part or offcut records
QTY_PARTS - Quantity of this part produced by this cut for all cycles of this pattern.
COMMENT - optional field to store narrative about the cut instruction

Some points about the cut record
Note that some cuts produce several parts with different item numbers because although the parts may have the same size they will be labelled uniquely. This can occur when cutting multiple sheets in a book where the parts on
different have different item numbers or where a crosscut of two strips side-by-side may produce parts with different identities.
The Sequence number allows definition of different parts in stack produced from same cut. Note, the cuts are not listed in cut sequence because it is necessary to nest the 2nd, 3rd and later phase cuts. The SEQUENCE number is
optional, and if not given then the cutting sequence should be determined by the saw or a post-processor.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                    9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                   Page 204 of 365

The PART_INDEX (if not 0) points to the PARTS_REQ records of relevant part or `X' + OFC_INDEX in OFFCUTS records.

The QTY_PARTS field allows for the display of the correct part quantities for duplicate parts. In a pattern with run quantity 20, and cut 6 sheets at a time, then there will be 4 cycles or books (3 with 6 sheets and 1 with 2 sheets). Say
the first part in the top left corner is a mixture of 14 parts item 1, and 6 parts item 2. The first book at the appropriate cut will produce quantity 6 labels of item 1, the second book also 6 of 1, the third book will produce 2 labels of
item1 and 4 of item 2, and the last book will give 2 of item 2. In this example, the CUTS records would show two cut lines, item 1 quantity 14, and item 2 quantity 6. The saw takes care of counting the cycles.

Note that the Sequence number will increment by the repeat quantity for that cut. In example below, CUTS 1,1,2 has repeat 3, indicating three cuts, so sequence number 4 implies 4/5/6 and the next sequence number is incremented by
3.

ASCII & database example

Notes in [] are for information only and not part of the file.

CUTS,1,1,1,1,1,500.0,2,0,0,"Job 1 - Ptn 1 - Rip"                [rip 2 strips 500 wide]

CUTS,1,1,2,4,2,800.0,3,1,14 [crosscut 500 strips at 800mm x3 >part 1 qty 14]

CUTS,1,1,3,4,2, 0.0,0,2,6                                       [and part 2 x qty 6]

CUTS,1,1,4,3,1,200.0,1,0,0,"Rip"                     [rip 1 strip 200mm wide]

CUTS,1,1,5,7,2,1400.0,1,8,20      [crosscut at 1400mm producing part 8]

CUTS,1,1,6,7,2, 0,0,X3,20                                       and offcut 3]

CUTS TABLE

JOB_INDEX                         PTN_INDEX                     CUT_INDEX                   SEQUENCE  FUNCTION  DIMENSION  .....

            1                                     1                                      1  1         1         500.0      .....

            1                                     1                                      2  4         2         800.0      .....

            1                                     1                                      3  4         2         0.0        .....

            1                                     1                                      4  3         1         200.0      .....

            1                                     1                                      5  7         2         1400.0     .....

            1                                     1                                      6  7         2         0.0        .....

15. VECTORS - INSTRUCTIONS FOR PATTERN DRAWING

VECTORS - JOB_INDEX, PTN_INDEX, CUT_INDEX, X_START, Y_START, X_END, Y_END
This table holds an optional description of the pattern as a set of vectors.
JOB_INDEX - Index number used to link this record to other records for this job

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                      9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                           Page 205 of 365

PTN_INDEX - This is an index number used to link this to the PATTERN record
CUT_INDEX - This is an index number to relate the vector to the CUT record
X_START - Start co-ordinate of cut in X (always positive)
Y_START - Start co-ordinate of cut in Y ( " )
X_END - Start co-ordinate of cut in X ( " )
Y_END - Start co-ordinate of cut in Y ( " )
The origin of the drawing is defined in the HEADER record. The x and y positions specify the distance to include the saw kerf, away from origin. So, a 10 mm trim would result in a vector at x=10, where if saw kerf is 4.5, then waste
removed is 5.5. The position for cuts producing parts must include all saw kerfs. Note, that unlike the CUT records where all dimensions are relative, in these records all dimensions are absolute values.
ASCII & Database examples
Notes in [] are for information only and not part of the file.
VECTORS, 1, 1, 1, 0.0,0.0,3660.0,10.0
VECTORS, 1, 1, 2, 0.0, 315.0,3660.0, 315.0 [rip 300 wide strip - note absolute dimension]
VECTORS, 1, 1, 5,5.2,10.0,10.0,315.0
VECTORS TABLE

JOB_INDEX  PTN_INDEX           CUT_INDEX               X_START   Y_START   X_END   Y_END
       1                    1                   1           0.0       0.0  3660.0    10.0
       1                    1                   2           0.0     315.0  3660.0   315.0
       1                    1                   5           5.2      10.0    10.0   315.0

18. SUMMARY OF DATA STRUCTURE

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm               9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                        Page 214 of 365

This section summarises the position and format of each field in each record type. The maximum length of each text field is listed in the comment column. The range of acceptable values for other field types is listed where applicable.
Please refer to earlier comments for a fuller explanation of fields.

Each field can be categorised by one of the following types.

DIM  Dimension. Number single. When working in millimetres these range from 0.0 to 9999.9. When working in decimal inches dimensions must range from 0.000 to 999.9

FLT  Number single. Floating point value.

IDX  Index. Number integer. These are integer values which are used to link records. For example all data for a particular job must have the same job index.

INT  Number integer.

QTY  A long integer used to store quantity. No quantity can be greater than 99999.

TXT  A text field used to store information

Note that spaces are not allowed in the material code, and any spaces will be converted to an underscore (`_') on import. Also note that material, part and board codes are converted to upper case on import.

The job records must have unique job index numbers starting at 1, and incrementing consecutively within specified range. The part, board and pattern records must each have their respective index numbers unique within the job, and
again be numbered from 1 and incremented consecutively.
