5766 Import PTX file for Parts and Boards into Cut Rite V7.x

The import/export of part and board records in the Pattern Exchange format (.PTX/MDB) has been modified to allow the use of all the part information (PARTS_INF) and user defined part information (PARTS_UDI) records.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 218 of 365

When importing pattern exchange files, all PARTS_UDI records are imported into the corresponding additional information box. System parameter settings for boxes will be observed, so imported text will be truncated to the box
length specified in the parameters. If a box is not set the text for that box will be ignored.

The import of PARTS_INF records is dependant on the necessary information box key letters being set. Each PART_INF value will be imported into the box with the corresponding key letter. These values will override the
PARTS_UDI setting. The table below summarises each PARTS_INF record and the corresponding key `letter'. Some new characters have been added to allow the use some fields. These characters are marked by a '*' in the following
list:-

The FIN_LENGTH and FIN_WIDTH fields are also used to fill out the combined finished length and width information box (key letter D). The CORE_MAT, FACE_LAM and BACK_LAM are also used to fill out the material
combination information box (key letter O).

The export of pattern exchange (transfer mode 11 and 12) also uses the new key letters above. The FIN_LENGTH and FIN_WIDTH fields are filled using data from the combined length/width information box if the individual finished
length and width boxes are undefined. Similarly, the CORE_MAT, FACE_LAM and BACK_LAM fields are filled with data from the material combination box if the individual core material and laminate boxes are undefined.

Importing Parts and Grain match templates from Pattern Exchange data

Users can now use files in the PTX file format to import grain match template patterns when importing parts/boards. Templates can be imported by adding PATTERNS and CUTS records to the PTX file.

The PATTERNS record should contain valid JOB_INDEX along with a sequential PTN_INDEX. The BRD_INDEX, QTY_RUN, CYCLES and MAX_BOOK should be set to `1'. The TYPE field should be one of four values.

5 = Create master part - divide at saw
6 = Create master part - divide at machining centre
7 = Cut parts in main pattern
8 = Cut parts in separate pattern

Each pattern of type 5,6, 7 or 8 a series of CUTS records are used to represent a template pattern. The CUTS records should contain valid part indexes. Grain matching information for parts will be added to additional information fields
provided system parameters have been configured for grain matching additional information.

A MATERIALS record can be used to set parameters for the templates. If this record does not exist, default `zero' values will be used. (e.g. kerf = 0)

On import template patterns are stored in a part list pattern library (<part list filename>.PTL). This pattern library is used instead of the main pattern library when (a) creating a cutting list (b) adjusting optimising results for grain
matching (i.e. inserting embedded patterns, appending template patterns). Each pattern in this library is named after it's index in the pattern exchange file (i.e. the first pattern has an index of `1' and so it's name is `1').

Users will not have to specify grainmatch info in a PARTS_UDI record. Instead, the patterns will contain valid part indexes relating to the parts. These will get converted on import to valid grain match info.

Thus the PTX file is like a partial optimisation.

For example a simple two part template (A,B) can be imported from the following PTX file:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 219 of 365

Note the use of the MATERIALS line to import parameters into the part list pattern library; parts 1 & 2 both have a material index of `1'.

There are no boards in this file so users will have to add boards after import. In practice other part sizes will also be in the list.

AF 4 June 2001

Pattern Exchange (PTX) changes

Importing templates from PTX - Improvements have been made to the import of template patterns from pattern exchange data (PTX). These changes fix a reported problem with recent versions where the fields in the template
information dialog are greyed out after import.

Users can use files in the PTX file format to import grain match template patterns when importing parts/boards. CUTS records are used to represent template patterns. Each template must have a PATTERNS record that contains a valid
JOB_INDEX, a PTN_INDEX relating to the relevant CUTS records and the TYPE field must be set to one of the following values:-

5 = Standard grain match pattern - appended to be cut on saw
6 = Standard grain match pattern - appended to be `cut' on machine centre
7 = Embedded grain match pattern
8 = Separate template pattern

The other fields in the PATTERNS record must be present but they are ignored.

A MATERIALS record can be used to set parameters for the templates. If this record does not exist, default `zero' values will be used. (e.g. kerf = 0)

The CUTS records should contain valid part indexes. Grain matching information for parts will be added to additional information fields provided system parameters have been configured for grain matching additional information.

Imported template patterns are stored in a temporary pattern library that will be used for grain matching optimisations after import. The template names will be the pattern indices. Reference: 7527

Importing PTX patterns with trim cuts producing offcuts/parts - Improvements have been made to the import of patterns in pattern exchange format. These changes improve the import of patterns containing trim cuts producing
either parts or offcuts. Reference: 7511

Importing part lists from a PTX - This now follows the same rules as importing a batch of part lists. That is, if the import option is selected from the File menu at the main screen the program provides an option to view the first of the
imported files. If the import option is selected from the File tree at the main screen the first part list imported is shown without any preceeding prompt. Reference: 7551

Import parts only PTX and board list files - If an import parts of a PTX file is performed and the PTX file does not contain any board information then no BRD files will be produced. The minimum requirement of lines in a PTX
file is PARTS_REQ and MATERIALS lines. Reference: 7572

Import PTX for parts - optimising & saw parameters - Importing parts list(s) from a PTX file uses the first available optimising and saw parameters for the part list rather than the Job Name specified in the JOBS record in the PTX
file.' Reference: 7583

Material parameters in PTX - Modifications have been made to the parameter values created in the pattern exchange data generated by the transfer to saw option (transfer modes 11 and 12). These changes fix the reported problem of
previous versions, which did not interpret material parameter differences correctly. Reference: 7597, 7595

Homag España CH03

This is a Panel Saw Link.

This link is for the Homag España CH03 for the Master TRIA 460 Controller. This is an unofficial file format since there is no way to communicate to the saw the cut type for each instruction. For that reason the following rules must
be followed to ensure cuts are made in the correct sequence. The 1st rule is that you must optimize for Labor which will limit the output to 3rd phase cuts. The 2nd rule is that the last strip out will be the first strip back in. With a

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                            Page 220 of 365

pattern with rips, cross-cuts and 3rd phase cuts, the rips are cut first. The final rip will be put back into the saw first for cross cuts. The last cross cut will be fed back into the saw first for 3rd phase cuts. The 3rd rule is that there will
always be an absolute cut when feeding a new piece of material into the saw.

Schelling HPO

Creates a customized part list for Schelling's High Performance Optimizer. Custom text fields can be setup in the machine parameters. For the complete list of variables, as well as how to format them, please look for 'Schelling HPO'
in the S2M Center help files.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                             Page 223 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                                                            9/25/2026
 Parameters

 Remove Line/Position

 This option allows you to remove the Positionnumber and Linenumber fields that are put at the end of the F 01 section.

 Output Board Database

 This parameter allows you to output additional files for output:
       .pdb
       .pla

 These files are added in addition to the .stk file

 Custom Text F[X]

 This allows you to provide additional information to the .stk file in the given section. You can use either literal text, or you can pull from a limited list of variables to display information.
 A variable is formatted as follows:
 {variable name}
 As an example, if you wanted to pull the Front Barcode Program to a section, you would do the following:
 Custom Text F10: {FrProgram}
 In the .stk file, this would give a result similar to:
 F 10 r008001
 The list of available variables is:

       RunID: Reference to [RunInfo.ID] linking the record to a run
       PartID: Reference to [Parts.ID] linking the record to a part record
       PartRunData.Output: 0x01=Selected, 0x02=PrimaryMachine, 0x04=SecondaryMachine, 0x08=DrillAndDowel, 0x10=TooBigForStdSheet, 0x20=TooBigForMachine
       GroupID: Reference to [Groups.ID] linking the part to a group
       FrProgram: Program name for the part front face operations
       BaProgram: Program name for the part back face operations
       FrMirror: Face operation are in a mirrored work field
       BaMirror: Back operation are in a mirrored work field
       FrBarCode: Front program bar code
       BaBarCode: Back program bar code
       FrWorkZone: Front program work zone
       BaWorkZone: Back program work zone
       LeBarCode: Bar code for horizontal machining on left edge
       RiBarCode: Bar code for horizontal machining on right edge

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 224 of 365

 ToBarCode: Bar code for horizontal machining on top edge
 BoBarCode: Bar code for horizontal machining on bottom edge
 IntBandBarCode:Bar code for interior banding
 ExtBandBarCode: Bar code for exterior banding
 Image: Binary graphic for this part used in reports
 ImageName: File title of part/label image
 RunMargin: Margin around the part for nesting
 RunPrimaryEdge: Primary part edge that rest against the point-to-point pins (1=left, 2=top, 3=right, 4=bottom)
 RunQty: Quantity of the part in this MachineSet Run
 CutWidth: Part width as cut on machine. Extended size: which includes pre-mill + EDX & EDY
 CutLength: Part length as cut on machine. Extended size: which includes pre-mill + EDX & EDY

Variables are case insensitive, meaning that {FrProgram} works just like {frprogram} or {FrPrOgRaM}. You can also use multiple variables in one section and mix in literal strings as well:
{FrProgram} / {FrBarCode}

Notes for Grain Matching

When using the `Close grain match gaps larger than x' preference in the S2M Center (under the Geometry tab), you may get additional cut passes for doors that sit under drawers, or in other situations. This output will use the Kerf
width (Cross and Rip) to determine what the gaps between doors and drawer should be and you can adjust those values to get the output you need to remove those additional passes.

Part Size: 6 x 21

CNC Output Path Explanation

There are 3 different types of output paths you can produce with Intelli-CAM. They come from a combination of the tool catalog (Centerline vs. Offset Path) and the machine catalog (Tool Center vs. Tool Radius)
   1. Centerline - Tool Center NO G41/G42
   2. Centerline - Tool Center G41/G42
   3. Offset - Tool Radius

CENTERLINE - TOOL CENTER NO G41/G42:

The outline path of the part is larger than the part by the tool radius. This path is to be followed by the center of the tool.

CENTERLINE - TOOL CENTER G41/G42:

The outline path is identical to Centerline - Tool Center NO G41/G42. The difference is it triggers to look up tool compensation values on the machines control.
Tool comp is important when you start sharpening tools. When you sharpen a tool it gets smaller. Tool comp allows you to inform the control that the bit has changed size and to compensate for the change.
For example: If you use a ½" router bit (.25 radius) and as a result of the bit being sharpened it has a new size (.23 radius). You need to account for this tool size change somewhere.

         >> With Centerline - Tool Center NO G41/G42 you would go back to the Tool Catalog in the S2M Center and enter the new size of the tool (.46 diameter) and re-output the program. Otherwise your part
         would cut out larger than it should be.

                  >> With Centerline - Tool Center G41/G42 you can enter a value of .02 at the control (.25 - .23 = .02) and the control will offset the tool path by .02 and correctly cut out the part.
It boils down to user preference whether the sharpened tool size is accounted for in the S2M Center or at the machine although you have advantages at the machine since if you need to swap bits you can compensate for different sizes
on the fly rather than having to re-output from the S2M Center.

OFFSET - TOOL RADIUS:

This path follows the outline of the part exactly. Thus if the router were to follow the path centerline, the part would be too small. This path must be compensated for at the control. If you are using a 1/2 bit, the
control will need an offset of .25 (radius). If you sharpen the bit you would need to change the comp value at the control to (.23). In either case the control will offset the path of the tool by the tool radius resulting
in the part being milled to the correct size.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 231 of 365

CncRunXXX.txt Text File Format

The CNCRun.txt file is placed in the Output directory for Primary and Secondary output and displays information about what was output. Following is the format specification for the file:

^Job : Job Name
^Description : Job Description
^Date : Monday, Jun 26 2000
^Parts : 1

r01b0001.dxf,Bottom(BO),304.00,889.00,19.05,3/4 2s White Melamine,5,1,0,B,0,

Field 1 - Program Name
Field 2 - Part Name (Internal Part Name)
Field 3- Part Width
Field 4- Part Length
Field 5- Material Thickness
Field 6- Material Name
Field 7- Cabinet Number
Field 8- Part Qty
Field 9 - Material Grain (0 - False, 1 - True)
Field 10- Program Face (F - Front, B - Back)
Field 11- Material Face-Dependent (0 - False, 1 - True)
Field 12 - Machine Tag Code
Field 13 - Machine Name

    Overview of the Tool Catalog

The Tool Catalog is used in creating and defining tools for use in your CNC operations. The Tool Catalog also allows an unlimited quantity of tools, of various types, in any combination, to be assigned to a user-defined "Tool Set".

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                              Page 232 of 365
                                                                                             9/25/2026
 The following is a brief introduction to the Tool Catalog's features:

 Toolbar

             New
         Allows you to create a New Tool.

                There are 11 possible Tool Types you can create in the Tool Catalog:
                    1. Vertical Drill - Used for Boring Part Faces/Backs.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 233 of 365

                  2. Horizontal Drill - Used for Boring into Part Edges. Will always be output as a Secondary Operation.
                  3. Flat Router - Covers the wide range of Standard Straight router bits.
                  4. Ball Router - Used for 3 dimensional routing and carving also used for routing slots with rounded bottoms or rounded inside corners (Flutes).
                  5. Bull Nose Router - Typically used for decorative bottom cutting.
                  6. Tool Set - A method for linking one or multiple tools together to perform a single operation. The primary purpose for a Tool Set is for the Raised Panel detail for MDF Doors. A couple of other examples may be to

                      preset a router bit used to create a bull nose to drop at the correct depth and offset for the bull nose. Another example may be to use a flat router to cut out a section then follow with a shaped router bit to alleviate
                      extra wear on the shaped router bit.
                  7. Dovetail Tool Set - Allows the definition of a Dovetail Tool as well as the Male and Female toolpath movements required.
                  8. Saw - Typically used for Grooving. A common accessory on Point-to-Point Machines. Also an Aggregate option on most CNC Routers.
                  9. 5-Axis Saw - Typically used for cutting Miters on Parts.
                 10. V Router Bit - A conical bit used for machining Miters in 2 ½ axis.
                 11. Shaped Router - Allows definition of Shaped Router bits.

            Copy
       Allows you to Copy the current Tool.

            Delete
       Allows you to Delete the current Tool.

       Tool Display Filter
       This option allows you to control which Tools are displayed by Machine assignment. The Any option will show Tools which are available for Any Machine. Selecting a Machine from the list will show Only Tools that are
       assigned to that Machine and/or Tools available for that Machine (Tools available for Any Machine).

       Tool Type Filter
       This option allows you to control which Tools are available by Tool Type.

       Tool Selector
       This option allows you to select which Tool is active for editing here.

See the Tool Catalog Setup topic for more information.

Tool Catalog

Each Tool, when selected, will display a window to the left with the properties of that particular Tool listed. Each of these properties are referred to as a "Field". The properties in the Tool Catalog are of greater significance when
outputting with Intelli-CAM. With Intelli-CAM ALL fields MUST have valid values.

              The following covers the Tool Properties Tab for Standard Tools. See the Standard Tool Shape Topic for information on Shaping Standard Tools, the Dovetail Tool Set Topic for creating Dove Tail Tools, and the Tool
       Sets Topic for creating Tool Sets.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                  Page 235 of 365

The following is a description of each field including a reference to the importance of that field keyed by the number of stars preceding the item. Three stars (  ) indicate the field is of critical importance, while one star, ( ) indicates
the fields of lesser importance.

General Tool Properties

     Machine - All Tools

Select the Machine to assign this Tool to. This list also includes three standard choices plus a list of each individual Machine you have setup in your Machine Catalog.

                The three standard choices are:                                                                                                                           9/25/2026
                Any - Indicates that the active Tool is available to any Machine in your Machine Catalog.
                Unavailable - Indicates that the Tool is unavailable (Not in Use).

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 236 of 365

Multiple - Indicates that the Tool is used on Multiple specific Machines in your Machine Catalog. After selecting this option you will be presented with a list of all of your Machines and you can simply
select which Machines this Tool is available for.

     Name - All Tools
       Enter a Name to identify the Tool. The name you give is for your reference only. In some posts the tool name will appear in a comment in the NC code next to the tool change.

     Tool Number - All Tools
       Critical for all tools except drills in a gang drill head. This is the ID that Intelli-CAM will use to match the Tool to the Machine in the Machine Catalog to the actual Tool on the Machine. For drills used in a gang drill head this
       value is ignored.

                     Some Machines (Post) do not use the Tool Number but instead use the Tool Name to uniquely identify the Tool.
     Height Offset ID - All Tools

       This is typically the same value as the tool number or the tool number + 100. This number refers to a preset value on the controller that defines the distance from the tip of the tool to the top of the spoil board or top of the
       Material. This is a value set by the CNC operator . It references the different heights of the tools above the work surface (In some posts this value is ignored).

     Tool ID - All Tools
       This is a unique ID Number assigned by the S2M Center. This value can be called from within your design package to assign a specific Tool to a specific Operation. For Example, within Cabinet Vision, in a User Created
       Standard you can use the Parameter ToolID equal to the Tools Tool ID number to make an assignment.

Tool Size Properties

     Diameter/Kerf - All Tools
       The S2M Center will use this size to match tools to operations. In addition it will determine for dado's if they are a single pass, rectangle, or pocket. This diameter should match the diameter of the tool on the
       machine - however there are some instances where you may not want them to represent the actual size. One example; if you always wanted your dadoes to create a rectangle or single pass regardless of the
       actual size of the tool on the Machine. In this case you would enter a Tool Diameter that matched the dado width for a single pass or was smaller than the dado width for a rectangle. See the document on
       Tool Selection Logic for more information. With a Saw Tool the Diameter field defines the actual Diameter of the Saw.

     Minimum Work Depth - All Tools
       Defines minimum work depth the tool can be used to perform.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                               Page 237 of 365

 Work Depth /Length - All Tools
  Defines the maximum cutting length of the tool. Typically, this is for a visual in the Tool Shape Tab. This information is not normally used by the Post Processor as Tool Heights are typically set up at the Machine.

      Minimum Work Depth and Work Depth work together to set an optimum range of material thicknesses for the tool. For example let's ay you want to cut your 16mm qand 19mm thick materials with your 9.5mm
Compression Bit and any material which is over 20mm thick you want to cut with your 12mm Compression bit. In this case your tools would be setup with a Minimum Work Depth and Work Depth as below:

Router Bit                                                             Minimum Work Depth  Work Depth
9.5mm Compression                                                      7mm                 19.9mm
12mm Compression                                                       20mm                34mm

 Cone Angle - V Router Bit

Defines the Cone Angle for the V Router Bit.

 Cone Angle - V Router Bit
  Defines the Side Wall height of the V Router Bit.

         Sample Miter Values and Required Cone Angles:
              80 or -80 degree miter requires a 20 degree cone angle
              70 or -70 degree miter requires a 40 degree cone angle
              60 or -60 degree miter requires a 60 degree cone angle
              50 or -50 degree miter requires a 80 degree cone angle
              45 or -45 degree miter requires a 90 degree cone angle
              40 or -40 degree miter requires a 100 degree cone angle
              30 or -30 degree miter requires a 120 degree cone angle
              20 or -20 degree miter requires a 140 degree cone angle

 Shank Length - 5-Axis Saw
  Defines the Shank Length for a 5-Axis Saw.

  Specifies the Corner Radius for Bull Tools as shown in the figure below.

Main Tab                                                                                                                                                      Page 238 of 365

 Lollipop - Ball and Bull Nose Routers
  This field is used to display the Lollipop Tool Shape for Ball and Bull Routers. The figure below represents the Bull Tool above with Lollipop set to Yes.

 Shank Diameter - Ball and Bull Nose Routers, 5-Axis Saw, V Router Bit
  This field is used to Display the Graphic for the Tools shank properly.

 Drill to Shoulder - Vertical Drills
  This will determine the final depth of any drill hole. Be cautious setting this value to true since the drill tip has the potential to drill deeper than the shoulder. This is a good option to use on a drill bit with a sharp drill cone angle
  and it is imperative the hole diameter at depth specified is the full diameter of the bit. This is related to DRILL CONE ANGLE below.

 Drill Cone Angle - Vertical Drills
  This number is only important if you are drilling to the shoulder. You have the option in Intelli-CAM when drilling, to reference the drill bit by the tip or by the shoulder. A value of 0° or 180° here sets the drill tip and drill
  shoulder at the same value. The smaller the drill cone angles the greater the gap between the drills tip and shoulder. On most standard drill bits the drill cone angle is 118°.

Tool Selection Properties

  Allow Auto Select

  This option will keep the S2M Center from evaluating and assigning this Tool automatically using Automatic Tool Selection logic. This Tool will still be used if it is specifically assigned to an Operation via your CAD
  application.

 Allow Auto Select on Stop Dado - Saw

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                         9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                          Page 239 of 365

  This option, when enabled, will allow a Saw to be auto-selected for Stop Dados. Typically this is unwanted as the saw will travel to its midpoint and overshoot the dado to maintain full depth along the dados length.
 Outline Tool - Flat Routers

  The Outline tool is the tool used to cut all perimeter cuts in a nest. It is necessary for one and ONLY ONE tool to be set as the Outline tool. See the document on Tool Selection Logic for more information.

 Down Shear/Up Shear - Flat Routers
  This field assists the S2M Center in selecting the correct Tool for an operation depending on whether the Material properties requires the minimization of Face Chipping, Back Chipping, or both.

 Through - Vertical Drills
  This value will assist the S2M Center in selecting the right Tool for the Job. If you have two identical Tools except for this value it will decide which Tool to use based on if the operation goes all the way through the Material or
  not.

 Diameter Tolerance - Vertical and Horizontal Drills
  This value allows a Diameter mismatch tolerance in case your Tool Diameter differs from the Geometry you would like to drill with the Tool. The most common use for this would be if you define your Drill Diameter in Metric
  but your geometry may be defined in Imperial.

 Direction - Saws and Horizontal Drills
  This option restricts a Horizontal Drill to moving in a certain direction to match the actual tool/machine restrictions. This will assist the S2M Center in selecting the correct tool for an operation. Some machines are orientated
  with the width along the long axis, while others are orientated with the width along the short axis. This allows Intelli-CAM to meet each requirement. Allows saws, for instance, to operate only on the "X" axis. If no tool can be
  found to create the operation it will produce an error message during output.

Tool Movement Properties

     Output Path - Saws, Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, Shaped Routers, 5-Axis Saw

       This will determine if the path of the tool is described from the center or the edge of the tool. This, typically, should be set to "Offset" for Intelli-CAM output, Intelli-CAM favors the "Offset" path. "Offset" is the preferred output
       for the greatest level of accuracy after tools have been sharpened. However, only some output methods support offset paths. For these output methods the tool path should be set to "Center Line". Check for your Machine's
       capabilities.

     Rotate Clockwise - All Tools

       This field in addition to the Material field "Climb Cut" will determine the Tool path direction. Think of the router bit as a wheel. If the path the wheel is following is the same direction, as a wheel would follow if rotated that
       direction, it is a "Climb Cut" otherwise, it is a "Conventional Cut". An inside cut will always be in the opposite direction of an outside cut. A "Conventional Cut" will always be in the opposite direction of a "Climb Cut". A
       simple rule is: If the Material is set for a "Climb Cut" an outside pass would be in the same direction of Tool rotation. Here is a chart to keep straight all the options: Remember the analogy to the wheel that will help keep the
       matter, including inside, outside, etc... straight.

                                  Material Climb Cut   Material Conventional Cut

          Tool Rotation  Outside  Inside (Dado)        Outside  Inside (Dado)

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                             9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                           Page 240 of 365

     Clockwise      CW  CCW                                                       CCW   CW
Counter-Clockwise  CCW   CW                                                        CW  CCW

 Max Depth Per Pass - Vertical Drills, Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, and Shaped Routers

For some CNC output, this will split an operation into multiple instructions so that a tool will make multiple passes to achieve the final depth.

This field should be set to 0 for a drill being used in a gang drill head unless your Post has been modified to support it. Setting this value to 0 will ensure the correct routine is called in the post for the gang drill
head. This is a good option to use with large drill bits and narrow router bits where the depth of cut may require excess strain on the tool. For Drill tools any value other than 0 will call a Pecking rather than a Bore Cycle in your
Post.

                     Drill operations terminology:

                          1. Bore: The tool makes the complete operation in one cycle.
                          2. Pecking: The tool creates the bore by making a series of plunges, each one deeper than the previous one, until the desired depth is reached.

                     If you receiving a "Pecking Not Allowed" or "No Pecking Allowed" message during output then that means your Post does not support Pecking. You can contact Cabinet Vision to see if your Post can be modified to
               support Pecking.
     Add to Through Bore/Cut - Flat Routers and Vertical and Horizontal Drills
       This value is very important; the machine operator can be very particular about this option. If the value is kept at the default (0) it may be a little difficult to separate the parts on your 1st nested sheet. Adding a small value allows
       for a cut slightly deeper than the thickness of the sheet thusly allowing cleaner part separation from the original sheet, this will leave a trace of the path in the spoil board.

Tool Nesting Properties

     Nest Gap Padding - V Router Bit
       There are many factors when determining the spacing required for adjacent parts when allowing miters on a nest. The thickness of the material, the angle of the tool, the through cut adjustment and the angles of the previous and
       next edge to the edge being mitered. All these factors are combined to determine the gap required for an adjacent part. This value allows you to add extra spacing. This value would be similar to the "Pad" in the Machine Catalog
       Part Spacing settings.

Tool Feed Rate Properties

     Feed Rate - Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, Shaped Routers and Vertical Drills
       This determines how fast the Machine will try to move the Tool through the Material. This is often a difficult field to find values for since we lack a standard for feed rate. The value represents Inches per minute, or MM per
       Minute. This can be even further confusing since some posts expect a unit but then convert the unit in the post. For example the MultiCAM post expects Inches Per Minute but converts the final output to Inches per Second. As
       this process progresses expect to see a standardized unit for feed rate. Until we get there you may have to experiment with this output. In addition the material will have an effect on the final feed rate. When testing take into
       account the % feed rate property in the Material Catalog.

     1/4 Feed Rate - Saws, 5-Axis Saw
       This determines how fast the Machine will try to move the Tool through the Material at ¼" Deep.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                              9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                       Page 241 of 365

     3/4 Feed Rate - Saws, 5-Axis Saw
       This determines how fast the Machine will try to move the Tool through the Material at ¾" Deep.

     Descent Rate - Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, and Shaped Routers
       This will have the same unit of measurement as feed rate.

     Spindle Speed - Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, Shaped Routers and Vertical Drills
       This is the speed at which the tool will spin. The unit is almost always RPM - Rotations per minute. Setting a value of 0 here will usually cause an error in the NC Code. A valid range would be about 4000 to 24000. It would
       depend on the Machine and Tool's recommended specifications. Some Machines will ignore this number and use a preset number for a Tool.

4 and 5 Axis Router Setup

     A or B Axis Rotation +/- - Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, Shaped Routers, Vertical Drills, 5-Axis Saw
       Amount tool can rotate on the A or B axis. A value of +/-90 allows a rotation of 180 degrees. Set to zero if your Machine does not have an A or B Axis.

     C Axis Rotation +/- - Flat, Ball, Bull Nose, Shaped Routers, Vertical Drills, 5-Axis Saw
       Amount tool can rotate on the C axis. A value of +/-180 allows a rotation of 360 degrees. Set to zero if your Machine does not have a C Axis.

Tool CAD Properties

     Pen - All Tools
       Defines a pen color when viewing DXF output in AutoCAD® or other 3rd Party CAD/CAM applications.

     Layer - All Tools
       This is the Layer Name Operations utilizing this Tool will pass to your CAD/CAM package. You can use variables to define a layer name.

          Valid variables include:

          {TS} = Tool Size

          {DZ} = Tool Depth

          {ZP} = Tool Z Position (Measured from work piece top/front face.)

          {ZB} = Tool Z Position (Measured from work piece bottom/back face.)

          {TC} = Tool Comp

          {TN} = Tool Number

          {PN} = Tool Pen Number

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                          9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                      Page 242 of 365
                                                                     9/25/2026
          {FD} = Tool Feed Rate

          {DR} = Tool Descent Rate.

     Diameter

       The S2M Center will use this size to match tools to operations. In addition it will determine for dado's if they are a single pass, rectangle, or pocket. This diameter should match the diameter of the tool on the machine; however
       there are some instances where you may not want them to represent the actual size. One example; if you always wanted your dadoes to create a rectangle or single pass regardless of the actual size of the tool on the Machine. In
       this case you would enter a Tool Diameter that matched the dado width for a single pass or was smaller than the dado width for a rectangle. See the topic on Automatic Tool Selection Logic for more information.

Tool Sets

Tool Sets give the ability to assign multiple Tools to a single Route path with one selection. You have the ability to assign each Tools offset, depth, and other items in relation to a predefined Route Path.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                            9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 250 of 365

Following are the sections in the Properties section for Tool Sets:

General

     Machine - Assigns the selected Tool Set to a particular Machine(s) from the Machine Catalog.

     Name - A name you enter to easily identify the Tool Set.

     Tool Number - This is typically 0 as each individual Tool within the Tool Set dictates the Tool used by the machine. However, if you want to assign a Tool Set to a DXF Layer on imported DXF Parts you can use this as the
       Tool Number for identifying the Tool Set. It is recommended in that case that you set this to some unique Tool Number.

     Tool ID - This field is not editable. Its value is a unique number automatically assigned to each Tool/Tool Set. This value can be called from within your design package to assign a specific Tool to a specific Operation. For
       Example, within Cabinet Vision, in a User Created Standard you can use the Parameter ToolID equal to the Tools Tool ID number to make an assignment.

Tool Size

     Diameter - Specifies the Diameter of the Tool Set. Establishes logic for Automatic Tool Selection. Only valid if Allow Auto Select is enabled.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                          Page 251 of 365

     Work Depth - Specifies the Working Depth of the Tool Set.

Tool Selection

     Allow Auto Select - This option allows you to exclude the Tool Set from Automatic Tool Selection Logic. This field will only be available if one of the Tools within the Tool Set has a depth reference of Operation Depth.
     Outline Tool - This option allows you to establish the Tool Set as an Outline Tool. This field will only be available if one of the Tools within the Tool Set has a depth reference of Bottom of Material or Operation Depth.
     Down Shear - Establishes logic for Automatic Tool Selection. Only valid if Allow Auto Select is enabled.
     Up Shear - Establishes logic for Automatic Tool Selection. Only valid if Allow Auto Select is enabled.

Cut For Glass Doors

     Cut For Glass - This field is not editable. Its value will change depending on whether you have defined a Tool in your Tool Set to "Cut / Glass".
     Follow Glass Shape - This option defines whether or not you would like your Tool Set, if set to cut glass, to follow the shape of the opening or to setup to pocket out the opening for a piece of square glass. In the images below

       the Rabbet on the Backside for the Glass to fit into is represented in red.
               Follow Glass Shape = True

Follow Glass Shape = False

       Rabbet Width - Defines the Width of the Rabbet on the Backside for the Glass to fit into.  9/25/2026
       Rabbet Depth - Defines the Depth of the Rabbet on the Backside for the Glass to fit into.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 252 of 365

     Router for Back - Defines the Route Tool to use to route the Rabbet on the Backside for the Glass to fit into.

Nesting

     Nested Part Clearance - This sets the space in between Doors and Door Parts on a nested sheet when the selected Tool Set is used. This value is added to the Part Clearance set on your Nesting Machine. For example you set a
       2" Nested Part Clearance (NPM) and ½" Part Spacing on the Machine, the space between two parts with a NPM of 2" will be 4 ½". Between a part with a NPM and one without, the spacing will be 2 ½".

Door Profile Types

     Outside Edge - Defines whether the selected Tool Set will be available as an Outside Edge Profile.
     Inside Edge - Defines whether the selected Tool Set will be available as an Inside Edge Profile.
     Raised Panel Detail - Defines whether the selected Tool Set will be available as an Raised Panel Detail.
     Route - Defines whether the selected Tool Set will be available as an Route Pattern.

Following are the options for Tools added to your Tool Set:

Tool Properties

    Router Bit - This drop down allows you to select which route Tool, defined in the Tool Catalog, to use in the Tool Set.
    Offset - The path a Tool Set follows is defined by a single line or path. This setting determines the offset Left or Right the Tool should be from the line or path.
    Depth - This value determines the depth the Tool should cut within the Tool Set.
    Drop Offset - This value is for use with under-cut type bits. This value instructs Solid how far away from the edge the center of the Tool needs to be prior to entering the Part to be cut.
    Cut For Glass - Select this option if you want the selected Tool to define the Glasses position if you are defining a Glass Door Tool Set. This will generate a backside Rabbet for your glass. See the Rabbet definition above.

Operation Properties

The Operation Properties presented here vary depending upon the selected Operation Type. Following are the available Operation Types and their associated Properties:
    Type - Rough - A standard, and the most common, Operation Type.
             Tool Side - This option sets the side of the Path the Bit is following that has bit pressure focus. See diagrams below.
               Looking at the Tool Set below the Raised Panel Cutter is set to Clean Left, this is because the Left Side of this operation requires a Finish pass and we would want the Left Side to be the cleanest possible cut. The other
               three Tools are set to Clean Right, this is because the Right Side of these operations requires Finish passes and we would want the Right Side to be the cleanest possible cut. See the second diagram below for more
               information.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                               Page 256 of 365

In the diagram below if you will notice the direction of cut in combination with tool rotation places the bits cutting focus on the specified side of the cut or the entry of the cut. Whereas the opposite edge is the exit of the
cut and therefore would be more likely to have tear out than the entry.

     Type - Engrave - ngrave is a special Operation Type designed for Engraving.                                                                                                                       9/25/2026
                       Make sure to use a V Router BIt for the Engrave Operation Type the path of the Engrave is calculated from the shape of the Tool. A Flat Router would not create a proper path.

    
               Tool Side - Previously Covered.
               Start Point - Standard Operations start in the Middle, however, when engraving typically you would start in the corner.

     Type - Pocket - Pocket is a special Operation Type designed for making Pocket Cuts.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                        Page 257 of 365

    Type - Clean Up - Clean Up is a special Operation Type designed to Clean Up Corners after a Large Diameter Tool Pass. Simply specify the Tool Radius to Clean Up after. Notice in the animation below that the Clean Up
       Cutter comes in after the initial pass and only cleans up in the Corners rather than making a complete set of passes to clean up.

    Type - Vertical & Horizontal Slats - Vertical and Horizontal Slats are special Operation Types designed to Cut Slats allowing the creation of Shaker Style MDF doors. Specify the Distance from the Route Path to the Start/End
       of Slat; this keeps the Engraver used for the Slats from overlapping your perimeter. There are two options for the Slat Spacing "Optimal" which will vary the Slat Spacing but not exceed the Offset value and "Fixed" will use a
       Fixed value for the Slat Spacing based on the Offset value. Simply specify the Distance between the Slats in the Offset Column.0

    Type - Finish - The Finish Operation Type, similar to the Clean Up Operation Type, is designed to allow you to assign a Finish Tool to clean up inside radiuses on the outside of a tool path. There are a few very IMPORTANT
       things to note when utilizing this Operation Type.

             The Outline Tool option is disabled unless the depth reference is Bottom of Material or Operation Depth

             A toolset will not be available for auto-select unless at least one tool is selected for Operation Depth

             Set the Nest Clearance to 0 unless you need extra clearance

             You have to manually set the tool size.

             The outline path should have a clean left edge.

               The animation below demonstrates the path of a Finish Operation Type.

    Type - Profile - The Profile Operation Type, similar to the Clean Up Operation Type, is designed to allow you to assign a Profile Tool to clean up inside and outside radiuses on a profiled toolpath.
             Maximum Depth Per Cut - This is the depth of cut used if the depth calculated by the Maximum Error exceeds this value. In most cases the default value should be sufficient.

             Maximum Error - This value is used to calculate the depths of cut and determines the surface finish. The depth of cut is calculated by moving the tool out to the Maximum Error then down till the contact point meets the
               slope. In most cases the default value should be sufficient.

             Clean Both Sides - This determines if you want both Outside and Inside Corners cleaned. When disabled it only cleans Outside Corners.

Reference Properties

    Depth - The Fixed Depth selection allows you to specify the Depth of the Cut from the Top of the Material being cut by a positive amount. Material + Depth allows you to specify the Depth of cut from the Bottom of the Material
       by a Negative number. See Diagrams below.

               Fixed Depth is a Positive Depth Value Measured from the Top of the Material this allows the Depth to stay consistent with a Material thickness change.

Material + Depth is a Negative Thickness Value Measured from the Bottom of the Material; this allows the remaining thickness from the cut to stay consistent with a Material thickness change.

                Operation + Depth gets its depth from the defined Operation. Use the Sample Operation Depth at the lower left to see how your Tool Set will be applied utilizing this option.   9/25/2026

Enhanced Tool Selection Logic for CNC Output

The following represents the automatic tool selection logic, which, the S2M CENTER employs when parts are output to CNC and is organized by operation type. This, in part, is made possible by the many
properties that can be assigned to a particular tool in the Tool Catalog. If you are unfamiliar with the properties that can be assigned to particular tools in the Tool Catalog, it may be best to review the information
presented in the Tool Catalog Setup topic in this help file before reviewing the information below.

Part Outline Operation - See Chart #6 at the end of this topic

Search tools for flat routers with Outline = "True"

   1. If found then select tool in this order
           1. Up / Down Shear bit that matches sheet requirements
           2. Compression bit
           3. Down Shear bit
           4. Up Shear bit
           5. "Expansion" bit (Up Shear & Down Shear = "False")

   2. No match - ERROR

Vertical Hole Operation - See Chart #1 at the end of this topic

Search tools for vertical drill bit with exact size match
   1. If found then select tool in this order
             Through bore setting matches hole requirements 1st drill with size match
             Check if Drill Bit is in Gang Drill Head.
                   If found - Check if hold position is in gang drill head limits
             If no match - WARNING - Search flat routers for size match
                   If found then select tool in this order to create hole in one drop
                          1. Down Shear bit
                          2. Compression bit
                          3. Up Shear bit
                          4. "Expansion" bit (Up Shear & Down Shear = "False")
             If no exact size match then search for flat router with diameter smaller than hole size
                   If found then select tool in this order to create pocket to match hole size
                          1. Down Shear bit
                          2. Compression bit
                          3. Up Shear bit
                          4. "Expansion" bit (Up Shear & Down Shear = "False")

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                          Page 259 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                                         9/25/2026
     2. No match - ERROR

 Horizontal Hole Operation - See Chart #2 at the end of this topic

 Search horizontal drill bits for a size and direction match
     1. If found then select that horizontal drill
     2. No match - ERROR

 Dado Operation - See Chart #3 and Chart #4 at the end of this topic

     1. If dado extends up to or past the edges of the part - Use saw
     2. Search saws for size match

               If direction is OK then use tool
               If direction is opposite then swap dado direction and use tool
               If direction is not usable then search for saw with smaller kerf
     3. If no size match then use saw with smaller kerf
               If direction is OK then use tool and create multiple passes
               If direction is opposite then swap dado direction and use tool with multiple passes
               If direction is not usable - WARNING - Search for router
     4. If no saw match Use router
               Search flat routers for size match and Up Shear / Down Shear match (Will Select Down Shear for a Dado)
     5. If found then create single path using router
               If no size match then search for flat router with smaller diameter than dado width
               If found and tool diameter is greater than or equal to ½ dado width then create 4-pass route using tool selected in this order

                    1. Up / Down shear bit that matches sheet requirements
                    2. Down Shear bit
                    3. Compression bit
                    4. "Expansion" bit (Up Shear & Down Shear = "False")
                    5. Up Shear bit
               If found and tool diameter < ½ dado width then create multi-pass route (starting from the outside and then moving inward concentrically) using tool in this order
                    1. Up / Down shear bit that matches sheet requirements
                    2. Down Shear bit
                    3. Compression bit
                    4. "Expansion" bit (Up Shear & Down Shear = "False")
                    5. Up Shear bit
     6. No match - ERROR

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                               Page 260 of 365
                                                                                                                                                              9/25/2026
 Cutout / Pocket Route Operation - See Chart #5 at the end of this topic

     1. Search flat routers for size match and Up Shear / Down Shear match
               If found then create single path using tool
               If no size match then search for smaller diameter flat router with Up Shear / Down Shear match
                    1. If found and tool diameter is greater than or equal to ½ dado width then create 4-pass route
                    2. If found and tool diameter < ½ dado width then create multi-pass route
                            If no match found then search for any flat router with smaller diameter then the route operation
                            If found and tool diameter is greater than or equal to ½ dado width then select tool in this order to create 4-pass route

 Import DXF Parts allows you to bring in Parts you have created in other CAD packages into the S2M CENTER.
 To Import a DXF Part:

     1. Click the Select button.
     2. Browse to the location of your DXF Part, click on it, and click Open. See the Other CAD Importers topic for other import options.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 267 of 365

                     You can use standard Windows Ctrl + Click or Shift + Click combinations to select multiple DXF's here.

   3. Click the Open button.
Once your DXF Parts are displayed on the screen they ready to be added to your Job by clicking Save. However, there are several options prior to Adding the Part to the Job you may want to modify. First, your Parts will appear on the
Viewing Screen to the Left.

By selecting each Layer Name in the list at the upper Right of the screen you can see that Operation highlight in the preview screen you will also be able to see below how the S2M CENTER has translated the Layer Names.
             Each identified Part or set of Operations will be displayed, when selected, either in Blue or in Red. If the Part or Operation is displayed in Red it means the S2M CENTER has been unable to determine the Operation Type.

       This typically means that the Layer has not been defined correctly. You can either edit the Layer Scheme for the S2M CENTER or edit the Layer Name within the DXF from this area.

Edit DXF Layer Scheme

This is where you define how your Layers coming from your CAD package are defined.

Toolbar

There are 5 options in the toolbar:
      Layer Scheme Selector - Allows you to select which DXF Layer Scheme to use.
      New Scheme - Allows you to create a new DXF Layer Scheme.
      Copy Scheme - Allows you to copy the current DXF Layer Scheme.
      Rename Scheme - Allows you to rename the current DXF layer Scheme.
      Delete Scheme - Allows you to delete the current DXF Layer Scheme.

There are three sections in the Advanced Screen:
Layers - There are 12 options in the Layers Section.

   1. Part - Specifies which Layer defines the Parts boundary.
   2. Outline - Specifies which Layer defines the Parts Outline.
   3. Vertical Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Vertical Boring Operations.
   4. Horizontal Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Horizontal Boring Operations.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 268 of 365

   5. Left Horizontal Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Left Horizontal Boring Operations.
   6. Right Horizontal Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Right Horizontal Boring Operations.
   7. Front Horizontal Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Front Horizontal Boring Operations.
   8. Back Horizontal Bore - Specifies which Layer defines Back Horizontal Boring Operations.
   9. Route - Specifies which Layer defines Route Operations.
  10. Dado - Specifies which Layer defines Dado Operations.
  11. Pocket - Specifies which Layer defines Pocket Operations.
  12. Edge Miter - Specifies which Layer defines an Edge Miter.
Keywords - There are 8 options in the Keywords Section.
   1. Tool Number - Specify the character that represents your Tool Number. Typically it is the letter T.
   2. Operation Depth - Specify the character that represents your Operation Depth. Typically it is the letter D.
   3. Operation Width - Specify the character that represents your Operation Width. Typically it is the letter W.
   4. Offset Left - Specify the character that represents if your Operation needs to machine with a Left Cutter Offset (Comp). Typically it is the letter L.
   5. Offset Right - Specify the character that represents if your Operation needs to machine with a Right Cutter Offset (Comp). Typically it is the letter R.
   6. Tool Inside - Specify the character that represents if your Operation needs to be machined on the Inside.
   7. Tool Outside - Specify the character that represents if your Operation needs to be machined on the Outside.
   8. Secondary Face - Identifies that the Operation is on the Secondary Face. Without this designation on an operation the assumption is the Operation is on the Primary Face. This allows you to define machining on both faces of the

       Part from one DXF drawing.
Miscellaneous - There are 3 options in the Miscellaneous Section.

   1. Decimal Separator - Specify what character you use as a decimal separator. Typically it is either the letter p or an underscore _.
   2. Part Face Up - Specify what character you use to indicate the Part is Face Up. By default it is assumed to be Back Up.
   3. Use Thickness as Depth - Set this to True if your CAD geometry includes a true thickness representation.

                     If there is a duplicate Layer name or Keyword a notification occurs next to the OK button and you will be prompted to fix the problem before exiting, copying or selecting another scheme.

                     The Part Layer should (but is not required) contain a rectangle representing the extent of the part. The Outline Layer should hold the part shape.

Layer Breakdown

The LeftEnd.dxf Imported above includes five Layers. Let's break them down:

             Layer Names are case sensitive.
Dadoes can be defined three different ways, as shown below, to be interpreted correctly by the S2M CENTER.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                         Page 269 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                        9/25/2026
 Single Line with No Width - The S2M CENTER will define the Dado to the Right of your drawn Line and the Width must be defined in the Layer Name.

 Single Line with Width - The S2M CENTER will define the Dado from the Center of the Line out based on the Width of the line.

  Rectangle Polyline - The S2M CENTER will define the on the inside of the rectangle based on the Offset defined in your layer name.

 Edit DXF Layer Names

 This utility allows you to edit the Layer name passed by the DXF. Simply type the new layer name(s) you wish to use in the corresponding New Layer Name field.

 Known Limitations

 Although the DXF Import reads a DXF file there are some differences between an output DXF and Input DXF that should be noted.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 270 of 365

1. One DXF file per part. It is common to have a Partition output both a front and back program. However since each DXF file is assigned to a part the DXF file should contain both the face and the back operations rather than
   having them split between two DXF files.

2. No mirrored parts. The DXF files input should not be mirrored. Currently the application will only rotate part 90 degrees or flip the part over if necessary.
3. Operation size is assumed to be the bounding box of the operation.

   For example, if you output the tool path for a dado as centerline, the geometry for the dado is smaller than the actual dado required since the operation will grow by the tool diameter.

   4. A DXF file can define quite an assortment of CAD entities that the S2M CENTER can not process. If the S2M CENTER can not process an entity it will be ignored and a warning will be issued. The following entities are
       supported by the S2M CENTER:
             Arc
             Circle
             Line
             LWPOLYLINE
             POLYLINE
             SEQEND
             VERTEX

match your CAD applications default output however each can be modified to suit your needs.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm

The material list will be filled with materials that match the part size. If there is no match a material will be created to match the part size.

BXFExt.ini

              The BXFExt.ini file is only required for the files with BXF extensiont, BXF2 does not require this ini file.

The BXFExt.ini is located in the installation extraction folder at \Support\BlumDynaplan\ and should be copied to the \S2M 2025\ folder. The BXFExt.ini file serves two purposes, localization and part orientation.

Localization

The data in the BXF file is in German, along with the part names. The .ini file can be used to change the part to recognizable part names. Note: The same part might have different names or may be in a numbered sequence. The
following is a sample:

       [LocalPartNames]
       LinkeSeitenwand - Seitenwand=Left End
       LinkeSeitenwand=Left End
       Rueckwand=Back
       *Rueckwand_2=Back #2
       *Rueckwand_3=Back #3

Orientation

The parts in a BXF file do not have a typical orientation like the S2M CENTER is used to, they are simply shapes in space. The orientation is determined by the size and position of the part. The part's face is based on the part's
location in the cabinet. By default all part faces point out. However there may be some parts, like a back, where the part should be oriented face in. To override the normal orientation of a part use the [PartFaceTowardCenter] section of
the BXFExt.ini.

              Parts listed in this section can be the actual part name or the localized name. Be sure to account for parts in a numbered series. Any non-zero value (such as 1) will change the part face towards the center of the cabinet.
The following is a sample:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                       Page 273 of 365

         [PartFaceTowardCenter]
         Rueckwand=1
         Back=1
         Back #2=1
         Back #3=1
         Top=0

S2M Part List

To use the S2M Part List CAD Import Link you will need to get the Import Link License added to your License by from your local S2M CENTER supplier.
If you are using a 3rd Party CAD Application, other than those predefined for the S2M CENTER, to create the DXF files you want to process then the S2M Part List is the Scheme you need to have selected prior to importing DXF
files.

When you output your DXF's from your CAD Application you can make the Batch Import of these DXF's more seamless by creating a CncRun.txt file (See the Creating an S2M Part List topic for more information). Simply change the
File Type in the Open Dialog to S2M Part List (CncRun.txt).

S2M Part List CncRun.txt File Structure

       Following is the format for the S2M Part List text file.
       Typical Header Layout

               ^Job : Test Job 1
               ^Description: My First Test Job
               ^Date: 08-18-2010 (01:03 PM)
               ^Parts: 5
               ^Unit: IN
               Banding,E,Oak EB,Honey,.01

                            The Banding listing is only necessary for defining Banding coming from Cabinet Vision if you are mixing an Imported List of files with existing Cabinet Vision Parts. This section is no longer part of the S2M
                      Part List Creator application and would need to be added manually. The S2M Part List Creator only defines S2M CENTER Material Catalog Banding through Banding Indicators.
       Header Layout Field Contents
               Job: Text
               Description :Text
               Date: Date
               Parts: Indicates Number of Part Line Items in the File
               Unit: Can be either IN (Inches) or MM (Metric)
               1 Banding (the word "Banding" must be the first field when defining Banding)
               2 Banding Code (A single letter code you use to identify the Edgeband used to build the Banding Flag described below)
               3 Banding Material
               4 Banding Color
               5 Banding Thickness

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                         Page 280 of 365
                                                                                                                                                                                        9/25/2026
         Typical Part Entry
                LeftEnd.dxf, End Left, 12, 30, 0.75, 3/4 Melamine, 1, 1, 0, b, 0,EEEE, 1,.5,.5

         Part Entry Field Contents
                1 DXF filename
                2 Part description
                3 Part width (x)
                4 Part length (y)
                5 Part thickness (z)
                6 Part material name
                7 Cabinet or unit number
                8 Quantity to be generated
                9 Grain flag (0=no, can rotate, 1=yes, cannot rotate)
                              The Grain flag is by Material which means a Grain and Non-Grained Material must exist and be assigned for Parts where this setting is different.
                10 Face Identifier (b=Back Face f=Front Face)
                11 Face Dependency (0=non face dependent, 1=face dependent)
                12 Banding Flag (Optional field containing the banding indicators for each edge.)
                              See the Banding Flag section of the Designing Label-IT Labels topic for more information.
                13 Grain Match Group (Leave at 0 (or blank) if not grain matched. This number can begin at an index of 1.)
                              When imported this number may be modified so it doesn't conflict with any parts that already have a grain match group.
                14 Grain Match Position X (Establishes the X Position of the Part within the Grain Match Group)
                              This field is required to have a valid value if Grain Match Group has a number greater than 0.
                15 Grain Match Position Y (Establishes the Y Position of the Part within the Grain Match Group)
                              This field is required to have a valid value if Grain Match Group has a number greater than 0.

 Importing Parts with no DXF File

 The CAD Import routine does not require a DXF file too import a part. If no DXF file is specified it will assume the part has no operations and will import it as a rectangle.

Edit Part Toolbar Tools

There are ten tools in the Edit Part toolbar:

   1. Return - Returns you to the main sidebar.
   2. Create New Category - Allows creation of a new Part Category in the Tree below.
   3. Copy Selected Item - Makes a copy of the selected Part/Category.
   4. Rename Selected Item - Allows you to Rename the selected Part/Category.
   5. Delete Selected Item - Allows you to Delete the selected Part/Category.
   6. Build - Each time you select a Part from the Parts List ALPHACAM draws (Builds) that Part on the screen. If you de-select the Build button it stops this which allows you to move around the Categories a bit quicker.
   7. Display In Metric - Displays your Parts in Metric.

                     ALPHACAM, unlike your Design/Manufacturing software, does not handle Unit of Measure changes on the fly. Therefore, when you Save a Part to the Part Library there are two Parts Saved. One Part is in Imperial
               Units and the Other is in Metric Units. When clicking the MM Tool it displays the parts "Alter Ego".
   8. Save Changes to Current Part - Allows changes made to the current ALPHACAM Part to be Saved to the Library overwriting the existing Part.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                  Page 288 of 365

   Design/Manufacturing software.

Radial

The Radial Type seems to follow the shape pretty closely.
Up / Down

Angle Down

       Angle Down Type has a problem on the Left Side of this Part.
       As you can see in the preceding images the Radial or Left / Right scan option would be the best choice. As stated earlier it really depends on the shape of the Part the Outline is being generated for. Experiment!
  10. Add Part to Library - Adds the current Part to the Part Library.

              Make sure that Parts you Add to the S2M CENTER from ALPHACAM are set to 0 as Top of Material and Depths are Negative Value equal to actual Depths for the Parts to be processed properly in the S2M Center. This
       must be setup this way regardless of whether you are set up in your Machine Catalog as "Mic at Spoilboard" or not.

Import Cutlist allows you to bring in a list of Parts in a CSV format for Nesting/Optimization. You can use your favorite spreadsheet application or design application to create a CSV that can be imported directly into the S2M
CENTER.

This utility examines the text file that you are importing and helps you ensure that the data is imported in the way that you want. You can use this Utility to define what each field in your Text file represents so it is imported properly.

 Toolbar                                                                             9/25/2026

 There are four options in the toolbar:

                                       Select Configuration

         Allows you to select which of your Saved data field configurations to use.

           Save

         Allows you to Save the current configuration for re-use.

            Rename

         Allows you to rename the currently selected configuration.

           Delete

         Allows you to delete the currently selected configuration.

 General Options

 There are four general options available to configure your Text file:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 297 of 365

Fields - This option allows you to establish how the fields in the Text file are defined. If items in the text file are separated by tabs, colons, semicolons, spaces, or other characters, select Delimited; then select what the delimiter is (Tab,
Comma, Semicolon, Space, Other). If all of the items in each column are the same length, select Fixed width.

Text Qualifier - Select the character that encloses values in your text file. When Solid encounters the text qualifier character, all of the text that follows that character and precedes the next occurrence of that character is imported as
one value, even if the text contains a delimiter character. For example, if the delimiter is a comma (,) and the text qualifier is a quotation mark ("), "Tuscaloosa, Alabama" is imported into one field as Tuscaloosa, Alabama.

Dimensions - Select whether the file you are importing is Inches or Metric.

Start Import at Line - Type or select a line number to specify the first line of the data that you want to import.

Import Preview Area

In this area you will see all of the columns that the S2M Optimizer has available for your Text file. Click the column header of the column(s) that you do not want Ignored and set its type. There are eleven options:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                                    9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 298 of 365

Ignore - Will ignore this column when importing.
Name - The Part's Name/Description.
Width - The Part's Width.
Length - The Part's Length.
Thickness - The Part's Thickness.
Quantity - The Part's Quantity.
Material - The Material the Part will be cut from.
Grain - Establishes if the Part is Grained or not.
Assembly - The Name/Description of the Assembly the Part is used in.
Assembly Number - The Assembly Number of the Assembly the Part is used in.
Banding - The Banding Flag for the Part. The Banding Flag should only include Banding Codes you have defined either in your design package (typically upper case letters) or within the S2M CENTER Material Catalog (lower case
letters). 0 (zero) would equal an Edge with no Banding.

This option allows you to start a Team Viewer desktop sharing session with an S2M CENTER Technician. Use this option when instructed by your S2M CENTER Technician and provide the displayed ID and Password.

        Zoom Group

The Zoom Group has three options:

        Window

The Zoom Window option is used for increasing the size of an area or Part on your screen to see a detail or examine a condition. By clicking on the Zoom Window tool, then clicking in the center of the area you would like to Zoom In
to, and, while holding the mouse button down, dragging the cursor diagonally across the item you wish to Zoom In on, finally releasing the mouse button at the end of the area, your screen will Zoom In.
You can also change the Window from Center out to Corner to Corner in Preferences | Tools Tab.

     Out

The Zoom Out option isn't a click and drag task, instead, simply clicking the Zoom Out option will incrementally Zoom the drawing out.

    All

The Zoom All option will restore the object size to the original size and re-center the object in the process.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                                             9/25/2026

This area displays your Sheet/Pattern layouts.

If a part can be rotated (not saw output, not a grain match part/block, not viewing the 6th face). A point of origin (dark green) and a rotation point (light green) will appear on the part. When the mouse is near the rotation point
 the cursor will change to indicate the part can be rotated.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026

Click the mouse once to start rotation, click again to finish.
              Whenever the rotated part is near 0, 90, 180, or 270, the part will snap to that angle.

The lower Section is the Part List and Clipboard area. The Parts on the active sheet are listed in the lower section of the screen. There are several columns of information which you can sort the list by clicking on the column heading. A
Part selected from either the Pattern are or Part list area will cause that Part to be highlighted in both Views.
Any items Cut or Copied via a Part Right Click will be listed on the clipboard . Simply drag any Part in the Clipboard to the Sheet where you want it placed.

In the Parts List/Clipboard area you have several tools you can use to modify Parts within the Nest:

   Part List View - Shows all Parts on the current Pattern in the list view.
   Clipboard View - Shows a list of all Copied Parts.

       Show 6th Face/Show 1st Face - Allows you to flip the Pattern over to see the 6th Face/1st Face operations if there is a 6th Face option on the selected Pattern.
   Rotate Clockwise - Rotates the selected Part clockwise 90 degrees
   Rotate Counter Clockwise - Rotates the selected Part counter clockwise 90 degrees.
   Rotate 180 - Rotates the selected Part 180 degrees.
   Flip Part - This option will flip the selected Part. This action will typically create either a secondary program, modify the secondary program, or modify the 6th face of the sheet.

   Material Catalog

When first entering the Material Catalog in the S2M CENTER you will see a screen similar to the one below with no Materials.

If you are using CABINET VISION, the first thing you will want to do is choose your Materials too display. To do this click the Show Materials from CV  tool in the toolbar. Upon entering the Show Materials from CV tool you
will be presented with the following screen.

Place a check mark to each Material you would like to display in the list.

Simply click Show once you have checked off those Materials you would like to Import. You should notice that once you click Import each Material you selected is removed from the list. This will make it easier to check if you missed
any.

Simply click Close to return to the Material Catalog. You will notice it is now populated with your Materials.
