Material Properties

 Let's take a look at the Material Properties area.

 The first thing you should make note of is that the Product the Material was imported is notated in the header.
 There are four sections in the Material option section:

       Product Properties - There are five options here:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                              Page 318 of 365

       Name - Enter a unique name to identify the Material here.
       Description - Enter a Material description here.
       Type - Allows you to set the Materials Type.
       Grain - Select Yes/No to choose whether or not to limit part rotation on the Material due to grain.
       Cost - Enter the Cost of this Material at the specified size here.
 Size - There are three options here:

       Width - Enter the Materials sheet Width here.
       Length - Enter the Materials sheet Length here.
       Thick - Enter the Materials Thickness here.
 Face Properties - There are five options here:

       Face Surface - Description of the Materials Face Surface.
       Back Surface - Description of the Materials Back Surface.
       Edge Surface - Description of the Materials Edge Surface.
       Clean Face - If Yes, the S2M Center will automatically select the proper Tool to minimize the blowout on the Material's Face.
       Clean Back - If Yes, the S2M Center will automatically select the proper Tool to minimize the blowout on the Material's Back.
 Tool Movement - There are four options here:

 Climb Cut - Use this option to specify whether to utilize a Climb or Conventional Cut. Typically, when cutting Hardwoods you will receive the cleanest edge utilizing a Climb Cut. When cutting Plywoods you will receive
    the cleanest edge utilizing a Conventional Cut.

 Feed Rate % - A percentage override to the Feed Rate for the Outline Tool when used with this Material.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                 9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                              Page 319 of 365

             Spindle Speed % - A percentage override to the Spindle Speed for the Outline Tool when used with this Material.
             Max Depth Per Pass - An override to the Max Depth per Pass for the Outline Tool when used with this Material.

Field Modification Options

The graphic below shows a highlighted field. There are different methods to move and size a field.
   1. You can use the fields X and Y to move the field.
   2. You can use the fields Width and Height to stretch or shrink the field.
   3. Alternatively you may grab the center handle (Yellow Dot) to move the field or the (4) corner handles (Yellow Dots) to stretch or shrink the field.

Field Definitions                                                                                                                     File Description
                                             Simply enter the desired text into the "Sample Text" box. (Text and/or Variables)
                                 Field Name  This will generate a 2D Barcode based on what is entered in Sample Text. (Text and/or Variables)
  Text                                       This will generate a 2D Barcode based on what is entered in Sample Text. (Text and/or Variables)
  2D Barcode Data Matrix                     This will generate a 2D Barcode based on what is entered in Sample Text. (Text and/or Variables)
  2D BarCode PDF417                          Displays Custom Part Data as defined when using ALPHACAM to create the xLabel output
  2D BarCode QR ISO                          Displays the parts edge band code. (See below for definition)
  AC Custom Part Data 01 - 25                Displays the Banding Colors.
  Banding: Code                              Displays the Banding Colors and Materials.
  Banding: Colors                            Displays the Barcode for the Exterior Banding
  Banding: Colors + Materials                Displays the parts edge band flags (See below for definition)
  Banding: Exterior Barcode                  Displays the Barcode for the Interior Banding
  Banding: Flags                             Displays all Banding used on the Part. See below for more information.
  Banding: Internal Barcode                  Displays the Band Materials.
  Banding: List                              Displays the Cabinet Comment
  Banding: Materials                         Displays the Cabinet Depth

  Cabinet: Description

Main Tab                                     Displays the Cabinet Height                                                                               Page 328 of 365
                                             Displays the Cabinet Name                                                                                        9/25/2026
   Cabinet: Height                           Displays the Cabinet Number
   Cabinet: Name                             Displays the Cabinet Width
   Cabinet: Number                           Displays the Part's Index on the panel.
   Cabinet: Width                            Displays the primary CNC barcode (See below for definition)
   CNC: Panel Layout Part Index              Displays the tag for the primary barcode machine
   CNC: Primary Barcode                      Displays the primary Face Up / Down position
   CNC: Primary Barcode Machine Tags         Displays the machine that will perform the primary machining
   CNC: Primary Face Up / Down               Displays if the part is a mirrored part for the primary machine
   CNC: Primary Machine                      Symbol Graphic displaying the orientation of the part on the primary machine. (See below for definition)

   CNC: Primary Orientation Symbol           Displays the primary program name machine tags.
   CNC: Primary Program Name                 Displays the zone in which to place the part on the primary machine
   CNC: Primary Program Name Machine Tags    Displays the tags for the primary zone machines..

Changes in Version 6

In Version 6 the Fields utilizing Banding Letter were removed to be replaced with Banding: List. The existing fields on your Labels were converted to new filter fields.
Banding: B Material is now {M}[Filter:B]
The new variables are:

{I} Or {Indicator}
                     {Tag}

{C} Or {Color}
{M} Or {Material}
{T} Or {Thick}

Now you can format the banding field anyway you like using those variables:
I.e.: {T} - {M}: in the color of {C}
In addition you can add either a complex filter [Filter:E|I|D] or a Simple Filter [Filter:B]
This allows for superior flexibility and optimizing label real estate.

Specifying Edge Banding on your Label

Banding: Code

The following characters can and may be displayed in the Banding: Code:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                                     9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                    Page 331 of 365

L              Length

W              Width

E              Exterior Banding

I              Interior Banding

D              Door Banding

A              Arc/Radius

Here are some examples of simple codes:
1LE - Band 1 Length with Exterior Band
2LE1WI - Band 2 Lengths with Exterior Band and 1 Width with Interior Band
For shaped parts when there is more than 1 side, you will see a dimension represented to indicate which edge gets banding applied.
Imagine a 34" end panel with a 4" toe notch:
1LE301LI - Band the 30" Length with Exterior and the Full Length with Interior
A part with a radius may look something like this:
1WE241A6E - Band the 24" Width and the 6" Arc/Radius with Exterior band

Banding: Flag

Another alternative to the Banding: Code is the Banding: Flag. The banding flag begins with the number of sides for a part followed by what each side will be banded with.

   N           No Banding

   E           Exterior Banding

   I           Interior Banding

   D           Door Banding

B-Z            Misc (B-Z) Banding

6NNENNN - In this sample part, starting from the reference point and going clockwise you get None, None, Exterior, None, None, None.
This type of coding can be quite confusing on complicated parts. The code and/or flag for the part below (24" x 36") with an edited shape would be:
CODE: 1W19E1W5E1L27E1A5E1W16E1L4E1W4E
FLAG: 8EEEEEEEE
To simplify the edge banding instructions to machine operators, you can use the Part: Graphic field on your Label. Each edge of the part will be marked with either an E (Exterior), I (Interior), or D (Door), as demonstrated in the
graphic below. With this method you would not need to display either the Banding: Code or the Banding: Flag fields.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                       9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 332 of 365

Primary & Secondary Programs

There is a difference in the definition of Primary and Secondary programs depending on the types of machinery you utilize.
Cell Based Manufacturing (Saw & Point-to-Point)
In the Cell Based Manufacturing model you cut parts to size on a Saw and then machine on your Point-to-Point. In this situation the Primary program is run first on the Back (Interior) side of the part. After this the Secondary program
is run on the Face (Exterior) side of the part. This is fairly straightforward.
In Cell Based manufacturing you would need to specify both a Primary program/bar-code as well as a Secondary program/bar-code to include all machining necessary on your Label.
Nested Based Manufacturing
In the Nested Based Manufacturing model you typically utilize one machine to do all of your face machining operations. When nesting in the S2M CENTER the program number that is generated is for a whole sheet of parts (Sheet
program) therefore no individual program number is generated for the Back (Interior) sides of the part. In this case the Primary program is for the Face (Exterior) side of the part. In this method of production there would be no
Secondary program since both faces of the parts are being covered by the Sheet program and then by the Primary program.
In Nested Based manufacturing you would only need to specify a Primary program/bar-code to include all machining necessary on your Label.

Generating +Label Output

+Label output is all generated on the S2M CENTER station. The process is outlined below:
   1. Click on Utilities Tab | +Label Setup.
   2. The first tab upon entering +Label Setup is Jobs. This screen shows currently active and inactive +Label jobs. You can remove any of these jobs from the system by simply clicking the check box beside the job and clicking the
       "Remove Selected Jobs" button.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 338 of 365

                  You can also select the Job Type from the Jobs Tab. The first option is "Linked (Small)" this +Label Job type requires the psnc.mdb database file be located in the same folder that you are outputting the +Label Job to.
           The second option "Self-Contained (Large)" creates a stand-alone +Label file that can be used on any +Label station and does not require a machine database for support. Although a bit larger it is the most stable +Label
           job file as it contains your Label designs and requires no linking back to the S2M CENTER.

                  An additional capability is the ability to control the Unit of Measure for the +Label. Click the Unit of Measure buttons at the lower left to control this.
3. Click OK to exit +Label Setup.
4. Optimize your jobs as you normally would.
5. Once on the Pattern screen, output your NC instructions as normal.
6. Enable the "Output to +Label" option. Type in the name of the +Label job. You may want to give the job a name that will tie back to your job name or number. This is the name the +Label operator will select to start processing

   the job.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                            Page 339 of 365

     7. Click on Run.                                                                                                                               9/25/2026
 You have now successfully output to the +Label Station. See the Topic on "Using +Label in Production" to learn how to access and use this output.

+Label App Feature List

   1. Name of the Job currently open in the +Label app.
   2. Clicking the View option allows you to View the Label for the currently selected Part.
   3. Laser - Turns on/off the Laser Pattern Generator. See the Running A Laser topic for more information.
   4. Mark Job Complete - This option does two things:

           a. When selected this option will remove the Job from being visible at the Label-IT station.
           b. This option at the same time will mark the Label-IT job Done on the S2M CENTER station in the front office.
   5. 1 Label - Prints the selected label.
   6. On Part Select - Prints the selected label.
   7. Sheet - Will print Labels for all Parts on the active Sheet.
   8. Sheet of Labels - Will print sheets of labels for the entire job.
   9. Patterns included in the selected Job.
  10. Current selected part.
           a. Selected parts that have not had labels printed for them are highlighted in green.
           b. Parts that are not selected and that have not had labels printed for them are white.
           c. Selected parts that have had labels printed for them are highlighted red.
           d. Parts that are not selected that have had labels printed for them are highlighted in blue.
           e. This field also has a drop down arrow which will show you the Parts below the current Part if you are Booking (Stack Cuts) using a Panel Saw.

  11. The Composites button allows you to view either the sub-part or the composite part. The icon will indicate the current view. Any time a Sub-Part label is printed the composite part label will be printed as well. The button
       will be disabled if the selected Part is not part of a Composite.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                          Page 342 of 365

   12. Selected Patterns material. This text will also indicate how to "load" one sided Materials on the machine like the Pattern Print outs from the Pattern area.

  13. Selected Sheet's location and quantity.
  14. Clicking the |< button moves you to the first Sheet/Label in the selected Pattern.
  15. Clicking the >| button moves you to the last Sheet/Label in the selected Pattern.
  16. Clicking the < button moves you to the previous Sheet/Label in the selected Pattern. You can also simply swipe right for the previous Part/Label if you have touch screen.
  17. Clicking the > button moves you to the next Sheet/Label in the selected Pattern. You can also simply swipe left for the next Part/Label if you have touch screen.
  18. Clicking the Print option prints the Label or Sheet, depending on your Preference, for the currently selected part.
  19. Sheet program bar-code.
  20. Sheet program name.

           a. This name matches the name of the program generated by Intelli-CAM for the Router.

Label Placement

Placing your Labels oriented properly on the part is extremely important in your success with Label-IT. There is a built in feature to help insure that you orient the Label properly onto your part. The graphic below shows a part with a
small dot. This dot represents how the Label needs to be oriented on the part. If the Label is placed properly on the part then the machine operators can look at simple graphics on the Label that will instruct them on how to orient the
part on the machine as well as which edges to edge band and which edges receive boring on your Drill & Dowel machine. The small line, within the circle, points to the top of the Label.

       Machine. This means it is important that the person responsible for Label Placement has the Label-IT station in his work area.
There are four possibilities:

      Label Up - Label would be oriented on the part as follows

       Label Down - Label would be oriented on the part as follows:  9/25/2026

The symbol below (Black & White Encircled Arrow) points to the back placement pins on the machine, typically along the X-axis. The below graphic representing the machine table and a part demonstrates this.

Additional Key-words: Metric, Imperial, MM , Units, Measure, Inches

Running A Laser

There are several Laser Pattern Generators on the market such as Carter Laser Products, Z-Laser, and SL Laser.
These Lasers can display the Nest Pattern on your sheet stockpile so that Labels can be pre-applied to your sheet stock before loading into your machine for processing. All of these use an HPGL file. To generate HPGL output in the
form of the file Laser.hgl for the Laser to read you will need to add a String Value in the Registry.

                      [HKEY_CURRENT_USER\SOFTWARE\Hexagon\CABINET VISION\S2M 2025\Label IT]
                      LaserRun="C:\Cabinet Vision\RunLaser.bat"

                             Make sure to backup your Windows Registry prior to making changes. It is highly recommended to not modify your Windows Registry unless you are familiar with the process. Windows and/or S2M CENTER
                      corruption could occur by making an invalid entry.
You can name the bat file anything you like. In the bat file you will need to add code to initialize the laser. This is typically one or two lines of code but since it is different between lasers it is not hard coded. Also in the bat file you will
want to make reference to the "Laser.HGL" file located in the \S2M 2025 directory. Below is a sample BAT file:
               MODE COM1:9600,N,8,1,P

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 349 of 365

               copy c:\Cabinet Vision\S2M 2025\laser.hgl com1
When you select the option Turn Laser ON, the program will create the Laser.HGL file then call then RunLaser.bat file. The Laser.HGL file is output whenever you navigate to a new sheet. At this point it will draw all the part outlines
on the sheet. It is also reset every time you print a label. In this case it shows just the outline of the part when the label is to be applied. The concept is you can print the labels one at a time before the sheet is milled then start the router.

Real-Time Labeling

When using Real-Time Labeling on your Machine (On Board Machine Labeling Solution). Following are the available Label Applicator Machines:

None

Use this option if you are not driving the Label Applicator from the S2M CENTER.

Anderson

A proprietary output produced for Anderson Machines. The Anderson output consists of the following elements:
   1. The NC Code for the nest
   2. An *.lpf file for each NC Code file. This indicates the label position, based on the center of the label for each part and a rotation flag (1=0, 2=90, 3=180, 4=270). The Anderson machine has the ability to rotate the label 90
       degrees so all labels images are output rotated either 0 or 180. The rotation flag then instructs the machine if it should rotate an additional 90 to cover the 90 and 270.
   3. An *.csv file for each NC Code file. The *.csv file contains additional part information that cab be place on the label. Look to the .CSV File section of this topic for a field map.
   4. An *.zpl file for each part that contains the part graphic.

The *.anc (Nc Code) *.lpf and *.csv file should all have the same file name. For example: r0201001.arb, r0201001.lpf, r0201001.csv

Biesse

A proprietary output produced for Biesse Machines. The Biesse output is much simpler than the Anderson. Unlike the Anderson there is not additional part data that needs to be output; they will rely completely on the Label IT image.
The .arb (Biesse Nc Code Files) will be modified after they are processed by adding an addition section for the label position and zpl names as follows:

       [CUSTOMSEZ01]
       <LABEL>
       PEZZO=1
       X=100
       Y=200
       STRCUST=C:\LBL\
       FILERIS=R01P0001.ZPL
       PEZZO=2
       X=200
       Y=200
       STRCUST=C:\LBL\
       FILERIS= R01P0002.ZPL
       </LABEL>

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                               Page 350 of 365

       %
       []
In addition the [PARAMETRI01] section will need to be modified to include:
       ETC=1 KE=1024
       Excerpt from Biesse Setup Documentation
       ETC parameter must be set to 1 when the panel labeling must be performed using the information written in section CUSTOMSEZ01. If the parameter is set to 0, the PC will ignore the information contained in this section, even
       if it contains some values. When the ETC parameter is set to 1, it affects the value KE, which changes from 0 to 1024. Section CUSTOMSEZ01 contains the information regarding the labeling operation. This section must starts
       with <LABEL> and ends with </LABEL>. Each label to be sticked must be described in the program and the description must contain the following information:

               PIECE
               X
               Y
               STRCUST
               FILERIS
       The field PIECE is a progressive number of the label, i.e.: PEZZO=1 Field X is the position, along the X axis, where the label must be sticked (its number is in the PIECE field). It refers to the reference point of the applicator
       (the offset set in the field max_lung of the tool is added and shows the label used), for example: X=123,75 Field Y is the position, along the Y axis, where the label must be sticked (its number is in the PIECE field). It refers to
       the reference point of the applicator (the offset set in the field max_diam of the tool is added and shows the label used), for example: Y=361,84 The field STRCUST must show the complete path where the watcher will find the
       label file to print, i.e.: STRCUST=C:\ETC\ The field FILERIS must show the label file name that the watcher will find to print, i.e.: STRCUST=LABEL01.PRN

Biesse CIX

A proprietary output produced for Biesse Machines using CIX.

Robotic Automation

A proprietary output produced for Robotic Automation Machines.

S2M Labeling

A generic format exported by the S2M CENTER format which can be used by some machines. Following are the specifications for the S2M Labeling Output.
There are 3 files output using this solution:

.LPT

This is a reinterpretation of the data in the .PLP (which is essentially raw label position) to the position based on the machine's Point of Origin. For that reason the .LPT file is probably more useful than the .PLP file since no data needs
to be transformed.

.PLP

The .PLP is a self-documented text file. Here is a sample:
       Planit Label Positions - Version 3.0 Format: PanelLayoutID, PartID, XPos, YPos, Rotation x 90
       1,8,26.7248031496063,-6.25000000000003,0
       3,36,55.4496061496063,-6.25000000000003,0
       2,35,26.7248031496063,-6.25000000000003,0

.ZPL File

A .zpl file is basically NC Code for a Zebra printer. It has commands such as move to this X,Y point, and print "Job Name" with this font. Move to this X,Y point, draw a line 60 points wide...
One element of a ZPL file is an image. An image imbedded within a ZPL file indicates a block of points then indicates which points in that block will be black. We have observed 4 different methods for describing the image. The
image format can be set/modified in the registry with the following settings:
HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Cabinet Vision\NcCenter_12\CncOutput
ZPL_Format - Valid values are "Anderson", "Andi", "Biesse", 0,1,2,3
If the integrator needs to modify the ZPL_Format he should set the ZPL_Format to -1 then use the following settings:
ZPL_AllowCount - Do not use the raw format, use the count method to compress file size
ZPL_AllowComma - Use a comma to separate each line of the ZPL file.
ZPL_AllowColon - Used with allow comma, use a colon to indicate a repeat of the previous line.
ZPL_AllowCRLF - Use a carriage return, line feed after each line of the ZPL file.
ZPL_AllowRotate - Rotate the image 0, 90, 180 or 270 degrees to match the nested part.

Template.ZPL

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm             9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                               Page 353 of 365

Since the ZPL file is not just the image it is the full set of instructions to the zebra printer the you can setup a template to embed your graphic data into your existing file. If a template.zpl file is found in the output directory, it will be
used in the following manner:

       ^FX Output from Planit ^FS
       [IMAGE.DATA]
       ^XA
       ^DF[IMAGE.NAME]^FS
       ^PRC
       ...
       ^XZ
       ^FX End of job
       ^XA
       ^XF[IMAGE.NAME]
       ^XZ
The highlighted areas will be replaced with the image data from the S2M CENTER while the rest of the ZPL file is untouched.

              For rotated ZPL output the program will look for the template0.zpl, template90.zpl, template180.zpl, or template270.zpl. This will allow for different label layouts for rotated output. If the rotated template cannot be found it
       will look for the template.zpl.

     Offcut Manager

The Offcut Manager is used to manage the reuse of Offcuts. Offcuts are considered any scrap/waste from an Optimized Sheet, equal to or larger than the size set in the Offcuts Tab in Optimizer Parameters or the Offcuts Tab in
Material Properties.

Offcuts Tab

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm  9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                  Page 355 of 365

 The Offcuts Tab display any Offcuts available for use in Jobs.                                                                           9/25/2026
 If you are using a Material Handling System you can also select to Sync with it here.
 Following are the tools available in the toolbar:
 Print - Will Print the list of Offcuts.
 Filter - Will allow you to Filter the Offcuts based on the field you select (Material, Width, Length, Qty) on the following conditions:

         Equal to
         Not Equal to
         Contains
         Does Not Contain

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                  Page 356 of 365

 Remove Filter - Will Remove the active Filter to show all Offcuts.
 Set Columns - Allows you to enable/disable and reorganize the columns.

       In addition to controlling which items display you can also control the order in which the columns appear. Simply click and drag the item you want to move into its new position.

Bins Tab

              In previous Versions of the S2M CENTER it was a requirement to setup Bins for Offcuts. This is now not a requirement; all Offcuts, equal to or larger than the size set in the Offcuts Tab in Optimizer Parameters or the
       Offcuts Tab in Material Properties will now be stored when utilizing Update Offcuts from the Patterns View.

 The best way to explain Bins in the Offcut Manager is by example:                                        9/25/2026
 I would like to save any Off Cuts that I can get Standard Ends out of. Below are my standard End Sizes:

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                               Page 357 of 365

Upper Cabinets       Standard Base Cabinets                                    Deep Base Cabinets
300mm x 300mm            610mm x 725mm                                           760mm x 725mm
300mm x 457mm            610mm x 825mm                                           760mm x 825mm
300mm x 610mm            610mm x 875mm                                           760mm x 875mm
300mm x 760mm
300mm x 915mm

To save each of these sizes I would setup my Offcut Bins as below:

          Part Size  Length                                         To Length  Width               To Width

          300 x 300  300                                            456        300                 609
          300 x 457
          300 x 610  457                                            609        300                 609
          300 x 760
          300 x 915  610                                            759        300                 609
          610 x 725
          610 x 825  760                                            914        300                 609
          610 x 875
          760 x 725  915                                            2440       300                 609
          760 x 825
          760 x 875  725                                            824        610                 759

                     825                                            874        610                 759

                     875                                            2440       610                 759

                     725                                            824        760                 1220

                     825                                            874        760                 1220

                     875                                            2440       760                 1220

If we look at the first bin it is setup to capture Offcuts that I can cut my 300 x 300 Upper ends from. The way the Bin is setup with any Offcuts that are sized between 300 x 300 to 609 x 456 it is going to assign for this Bin. Basically if
I go to this Bin it is going to have parts that range in a size that I can get my 300 x 300 parts out of. The parts in this bin maybe larger, say 575 x 400, but the rule I am trying to establish is that it can be utilized in a future Optimization
to get a 300 x 300 Upper end panel out of.

The bins are setup to contain Offcuts of at least the minimum specified size - in other words they are used to group Offcuts into similar sized panel stock. If you have a number of Offcuts in a bin and want to add them to the
optimization, the smallest size of the bin dimension is used for the material size. The bin is basically saying "I have these pieces of material that are at least this size". When you add Offcuts to the bins, it does not store the size of each
part that gets added to the bin, it just increases a quantity of parts that are in a particular bin. So when adding the parts back to the Optimizer it uses the smallest dimensions of the bin. In other words a bin is a set of parts that are AT
LEAST THIS SIZE even though some may have originally been bigger.

You can also Print and Set Columns on the Bins tab.

    Setup Package

The Setup Package utility is a means to transfer specific objects from one computer running S2M CENTER to another.

Exporting a Setup Package

The Setup Package feature allows you to create a single file that can store and transfer multiple custom items for import into another S2M CENTER user's or S2M CENTER Tech's system.
The following list of items can be added to a package for transfer:

Labels (Label-IT Labels or Saw Graphic designs only)
Machines
Tools

     · If you select a Tool Set it will automatically import all Tools associated with that Tool Set.

This topic explains how to export a package and send it to Tech Support or another S2M CENTER user.
   1. Go to the Utilities Tab | Setup Package
   2. Select the item or items you would like to include in the Package from the list on the left side of the screen. After highlighting an item you want to add to the package simply click the "Add Items To Package" button in the lower
       left corner. There is no limit to the number of items that can be added to a package.

     3. After selecting the items to add to the package click on the "Save Current Package" tool in the toolbar.                                                        9/25/2026
                       In the graphic below we selected two Tool Sets and the Packager automatically imported the all the individual Tools that make up each Tool Set.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm
Main Tab                                               Page 359 of 365

   4. Select a location to save the Package and type in a Name for the Package then select "Save".
   5. You may now either close the Setup Package screen or, if you use MS Outlook or Outlook Express, you can click on the "E-Mail Current Package" tool in the top toolbar. This will attach the Package to your e-mail and allow

       you to e-mail the SetupPackage to Tech Support or to another S2M CENTER User.
   6. You may now close the Setup Package screen. You have successfully exported a Package from the S2M CENTER.

Overwriting Existing Items

Following are the options that allow you to choose how to Overwrite items that already existed in your system.

file:///C:/Users/celav/AppData/Local/Temp/~hhEA7D.htm                                                                                                                         9/25/2026
Main Tab                                                                                                                                                                                                  Page 363 of 365

     · All Matches - Overwrite - Will overwrite all existing items in your database with the contents of the Package.
     · All Matches - Use Existing - Will use all the existing items in your database rather than overwrite them with the Package contents.
     · Child Matches - Overwrite - Will overwrite all existing children of items in the Package that match those in your database.
     · Child Matches - Use Existing - Will use all the existing children in your database rather than overwrite them with the Package contents.
     · Toggle Overwrite - Allows you to Overwrite existing items by selecting them individually. This will cause items in the Package to either overwrite the existing item or to use the existing item.
